# Significant Milestones In The Cryptocurrency Market

Let's look at the timeline of cryptocurrencies from the inception of Bitcoin and a few significant milestones it saw on the way.

2008

The first two cryptocurrency milestones occur. The domain name Bitcoin.org was registered on August 18. Then, on October 31 "Satoshi Nakamoto," the inventor of Bitcoin, released a document that kicks off the process: Bitcoin is a decentralized electronic cash system that operates on a peer-to-peer basis.

2009

On January 12, Nakamoto delivered ten Bitcoin (BTC) to Hal Finney, a computer programmer.

2010

Bitcoin was hacked on August 15, revealing a critical weakness in the system. Jeff Garzik, a Bitcoin developer, notes a very unusual transaction involving 184 billion BTC, stating: "We've got an issue here."

Later that year, the first Bitcoin sale occurred, establishing the first time that cryptocurrency had a monetary value. Ten thousand Bitcoins are exchanged for two pizzas by a Bitcoin user. These would be the world's most expensive pizzas since 10,000 Bitcoin is worth more than $447 million.

2011

Rival cryptocurrencies begin to appear with the introduction of Litecoin, Namecoin, and Swiftcoin. Meanwhile, Bitcoin is under fire after reports that it is being utilized on the so-called "dark web," namely Silk Road. Bitcoin's price skyrockets before plummeting again, demonstrating that no exposure is terrible PR.

2012

Cryptocurrencies became public awareness thanks to a fictionalized trial dubbed Bitcoin for Dummies in the third season of the US drama The Good Wife.

2013

Forbes dubbed 2013 the "Year of the Bitcoin." This was the year Bitcoin captured the attention of many hungry investors, commanding headlines at $1,242 per coin and driving gold prices to a session low of $1,240 per ounce.

Its worth soared as it gained traction at discussion tables, internet forums, and seminars. This rapid growth resulted in widely divergent perspectives on cryptocurrencies, with some seeing them as a hoax while others saw them as lucrative and appealing ventures. The reality is that both positions are very persuasive. Due to the extreme volatility of the cryptocurrency markets, succeeding years saw unpredictable changes in value and opinion.

When Bitcoin holders cannot agree on a new transaction rule, a Bitcoin "fork" occurs, and the blockchain physically divides in half. Two networks operate concurrently for six hours, with two distinct versions of transaction histories, resulting in an inevitable value decline.

Meanwhile, many governments are trying to determine the best approach to cryptocurrency regulation. Thailand prohibits Bitcoin trading, calling it unlawful. Germany's finance ministry would not recognize Bitcoin as an official currency but as a "unit of account," opening the door for a future framework taxing Bitcoin-related activities. The People's Bank of China has prohibited financial institutions from utilizing Bitcoins, resulting in another decline in value. The first Bitcoin ATM is opened in Vancouver, Canada.

2014

Exchange of cryptocurrencies Mt Gox, headquartered in Japan, falls insolvent and leaves investors out of pocket. Microsoft enables customers to purchase games using Bitcoin as an example of the currency's increasing appeal.

2015

The emergence of other cryptocurrencies, including Ethereum and Coinbase (which received $75 million in a financing round, the most significant sum ever raised by a Bitcoin firm). Bitstamp, a Bitcoin exchange headquartered in Europe, suffers a security breach after a hack and restarts trading a few days later, assuring clients that their money has not been lost.

2016

Cryptocurrencies continue to gain popularity. The number of Bitcoin ATMs has increased from about 500 at the start of the year to slightly under 900 by year's end; Uber in Argentina has begun taking Bitcoin payments, while the Swiss national railway and software company Steam are among new users.

The DAO (decentralized autonomous organization) was established in May - a stateless venture capital fund on the Ethereum blockchain and the world's most significant crowdfunded initiative. Users hack it a month after its debut, and a third of its assets are stolen. Consequently, Ethereum undergoes a "fork," dividing into Ethereum and Ethereum Classic.

2017

This was a watershed year in the history of Bitcoin, and this period permanently altered the way people viewed cryptos. Bitcoin served as an entry point for all newcomers to cryptocurrency in 2017. Individuals were only sometimes considering Bitcoin as a long-term investment. They were trading Bitcoin for altcoins and investing in initial coin offerings (ICOs), throwing away millions expecting to become wealthy.

The general public first became aware of crypto assets in 2017. The regulation was minimal; anybody with money could create and list a token on an exchange. Consumer safeguards were non-existent, and everyone became an expert at assessing early-stage, blockchain-based "investments."

This resulted in the initial coin offering (ICO) frenzy, in which everyone was misled by excitement and burnt by fundamentally poor investments. Bitcoin increased from $900 to $11000 in less than a year. This year was even crazier for cryptocurrencies since coins potentially increased in value from $1 to over $100000 in a single year.

Apart from this excitement, the highly anticipated "Bitcoin cash hard fork" occurred, dividing Bitcoin into two derivative cryptocurrencies: BTC and BCH. Japan enacts legislation allowing for the legal acceptance of Bitcoin as a payment mechanism. At the same time, Skandiabanken in Norway incorporates Bitcoin accounts and recognizes Bitcoin as an investment asset and payment system.

2018

In 2018, we saw one of the most significant price declines in Bitcoin's history (BTC). The altcoins market surged in 2017 during the Bitcoin bull run but collapsed in 2018 because of the downturn. This pattern demonstrated that all cryptocurrencies are inextricably linked to Bitcoin's trends. In other words, what reached an all-time high of $828 billion began to decline gradually. Consequently, interest in cryptocurrencies was waning in the market.

Numerous European countries collaborate on cryptocurrency legislation, and many continue to establish creative collaborations with high-street brands, such as Ripple's 2018 international money transfer app with Santander.

2019

Increased geopolitical tensions, global trade frictions, political instability, and uncertainty were just a few of the main themes that dominated the global market in 2019. As we've seen with the US-China trade talks, investors' expectations have fluctuated between an agreement and a full-fledged trade war. The Brexit issue has cast doubt on the UK's future growth.

Despite a turbulent market environment, Bitcoin outperformed other asset classes significantly in 2019. The performance demonstrates Bitcoin's effectiveness as a hedge against global dangers and stagnation, as well as a risk-on asset with a high rate of return.

Bitcoin may provide investors a 90% annual return in 2019 if they follow a simple buy-and-hold strategy. In 2019, the cryptocurrency sector was a completely different scenario. At the start of 2019, the Crypto Total Market Cap Ex-BTC was USD 52.66 billion. It surpassed USD 140 billion in mid-June when BTC reached a year high 13330. However, when Bitcoin prices fell, the Ex-BTC Market Cap lost most of its gains in 1H19 and ended the year at USD 55.90 billion.

2020

2020 has been an insane year for everyone, especially for cryptocurrencies. Bitcoin garnered media attention lately after setting fresh all-time highs for the first time since 2017. Global financial markets were shaken by turmoil as concerns of a pandemic-induced economic slump increased. On March 12, Bitcoin dropped below $4,000 for the first time in almost seven years, registering the most significant one-day loss of 27 percent in nearly seven years.

Bitcoin recovered to $7,000 in April, only one month before its third and final historical event - halving. After three months of stabilization, Bitcoin broke over the $10,000 barrier in October. Then, within two months, as investors were still discussing the prospect of Bitcoin falling lower, it surmounted obstacles of $12K, $14K, $16K, and ultimately $19K. In 2020, institutional investors and government sectors will undoubtedly benefit from Bitcoin's growth. JPMorgan Chase was the catalyst for the adoption. And this was the impetus for the 2021 cryptocurrency bull run.

2021

The bull run that began in 2020 lasted far into 2021, with experts and fans issuing lucrative price forecasts for major cryptocurrencies. Elon Musk's support at the start of the year accelerated the pace. All of the optimism surrounding Bitcoin propelled it to new highs and positively influenced virtually all the altcoins.

However, a hurdle occurred in May when Elon Musk changed his stance and said that Tesla would no longer take Bitcoin because it would jeopardize the environment. To make matters worse, a series of crackdown operations against crypto mining was conducted in China in May, the country hosting most of the world's crypto mining. This affected virtually all cryptocurrencies, and it seemed as if the so-called bubble had burst.

On the other side, half the Earth away from China, the year 2021 will be remembered as another South American nation formally accepts the first time Bitcoin. El Salvador has recognized Bitcoin as legal money after Congress received a request from President Nayib Bukele to embrace the cryptocurrency and eliminate all related capital gains taxes. Additionally, the President pledged to harness the country's geothermal energy by instructing a state-owned utility to construct geothermal facilities that harness the power from volcanoes for Bitcoin mining. The height for BTC in 2021 reached $68,000, another record high reached in October 2021, but closed the year below $40,000.

2022.

As we moved from 2021 to 2022, inflation, the energy crisis, and geopolitical upheaval owing to Russia's aggressiveness toward Ukraine lowered BTC and other top cryptocurrencies' values. Prepare for the 2022 Crypto Winter and shifting Bitcoin values. From January to May 2022, Bitcoin's price plummets. BTC dropped below $30,000 for the first time since July 2021 in May. Crypto winters are less defined than bear markets. Like a bear market, this period sees the cryptocurrency markets decrease steadily and sometimes sharply. Cryptocurrencies fell in Q2. Stablecoins like TerraUSD and Terrra Luna contributed to the crypto price drop. Bitcoin traded around $20,700 as cryptocurrency hedge fund Three Arrows Capital defaulted on a bitcoin loan and was ordered into liquidation by a court in the British Virgin Islands. In November 2022, FTX, a major crypto exchange, unexpectedly collapsed, further depressing crypto values. The conversation went bankrupt after its CEO, Sam Blankman Fried, was accused of misappropriating funds. FTX's financial difficulties and possible fraud prevented Binance from bailing it out.

The shocking collapse of FTX sent shockwaves across the financial industry, generating a domino effect in the cryptocurrency market and leaving investors uneasy. Inflation fears and rising interest rates impacted the market. As 2022 ended, Bitcoin's price dropped below $20,000, indicating the cryptocurrency market's health.

2023

The first half of the yeast saw BTC in the $ ’20s. We are now experiencing the gradual yet promising rise of Bitcoin's value, reaching approximately $30,000 when writing this chapter and igniting a glimmer of hope that the crypto winter is finally ending. Q1 had seen other cryptocurrencies following suit with similar price trends.

Experience the new heights of Bitcoin mining as we draw closer to the end of May. The recent surge in miner interest has caught traders' attention, who view it as an excellent bullish signal. While inflation remains a concern in the broader markets, there is growing optimism that the measures implemented by the Fed could lead to a gentle landing.

# Chapter 2: The Bitcoin Phenomenon

# The Story Of Bitcoin's Birth

On November 1, 2008, an anonymous entity named Satoshi Nakamoto wrote in an email to a cryptography mailing list with a nine-page white paper containing the first-ever mention of Bitcoin, calling it a "peer-to-peer electronic cash system."

"I've been working on a new electronic cash system that's fully peer-to-peer, with no trusted third party. The paper is available at Bitcoin.org/Bitcoin.pdf." Nakomoto said. The original paper detailing the math and specifics of Bitcoin is still available on the website.

As Nakamoto proposed, the system's main properties would be that the electronic transactions would be peer-to-peer and would not need to involve a financial institution. The plan was to be completely decentralized, meaning that the currency users would not need to rest their trust in a central authority, such as traditional central banks. 

Satoshi also expressed dissatisfaction that banks have repeatedly breached the trust of the people who deposit money with them by lending them money in credit bubbles while keeping minimal reserves. "The root problem with conventional currency is all the trust required to make it work. The central bank must be trusted not to debase the currency, but the history of fiat currencies is full of breaches of that trust," Satoshi wrote.

After publishing his revolutionary paper, Nakamoto released Bitcoin's first software and partnered with developers and coders online to improve it.

The first lines of code were posted to the Bitcoin blockchain on January 3, 2009, a few months after the original whitepaper surfaced. These lines of code, known as the "genesis block," are credited to the person or persons known as Satoshi Nakamoto.

On January 12, Nakamoto sent ten Bitcoins to Hal Finney, giving birth to a new finance counterculture, taking impetus away from banks and back to the people. At this point, Bitcoin's monetary value was negligible. Initially, users rewarded each other with Bitcoins for good comments in forums. The first "real" transaction occurred on May 22, 2010, when Laszlo Hanyecz famously bought two pizzas for 10,000 Bitcoin, which was about $30. This transaction would be worth more than $38 million at current prices.

The collaborative environment between Nakomoto and other developers continued until 2011, when, without warning, Nakamoto vanished, or did he?

Many attempts have been made to discover Satoshi Nakamoto's true identity, but with each name suggested, each turned out to be something other than the creator. However, details about the creator, such as Nationality and origins being obscure, made the currency trustworthy and free of any inherent biases. Bitcoin was invented amidst the aftermath of the 2008 financial crisis, and the crisis was an apparent motivating factor that led to its creation.

Many banks and other financial institutions failed worldwide and had to be bailed out by governments at the expense of taxpayers' money. This highlighted the fragility of the modern financial system, where the health of the monetary system was heavily reliant on banks and other financial institutions. We are forced to trust these institutions to make wise and prudent decisions with the money we give them. Too often, they need to carry out this fundamental responsibility adequately.

For most of its history, Bitcoin was associated with three overlapping communities: the small community of original investors, blockchain technology enthusiasts, and speculators just here to make money off this new financial instrument. 

Initially, Bitcoin was regarded as money with philosophy. Instead of a central bank backing the currency, it had programming and Nakamoto's whitepaper, both of which suggested skepticism about ordinary financial institutions and proposed significant theoretical advantages against the same. Bitcoin was structured in a way that exposed its ideology. The idea of a peer-to-peer money network was a disruptive one.

You don't have to pay a fee to a third party like a bank or currency exchange to transact money. But the original ideology is even more revolutionary than that: Bitcoin highlights how fundamentally bizarre the concept of money is. In some sense, money isn't "real" in the way a physical asset like a car is. Money is a human invention, a value token that makes exchange easier. But it works and is accurately provided that enough people believe in it, people fight and die for it, and establishments fall for lack of it. You can notice the influences of the banking crisis on Bitcoin's ideology.

It portrays a specific distrust of financial institutions. And in 2009, in an economy reeling in financial distress, many people were keenly looking for alternatives to the mainstream financial system that had failed them. As most people understand it, the banking system’s core is money. Of all the previous attempts at internet-based currency, Bitcoin was the one that came out as the best possible alternative to society's common anxieties around the traditional financial system.

Many investors see Bitcoin as a physical commodity, like gold. There's also the finite nature of Bitcoin, as there can only be 21 million Bitcoin in the world under the current protocol. More than 17 million coins have already been mined, and the rest will be released at a predictable mining rate. Central banks or politicians need to be able to make that system run faster or mint new currency to suit their political agenda.

# How Does Bitcoin Work?

Digital currencies like Bitcoin are a relatively modern concept, and there are crucial differences between digital currencies and the currently circulating government-issued currencies. Digital currency is similar to real currency, but they are not issued by central banks nor financially backed by the state as digital money. Cryptocurrency issuing is decentralized and is not decided by politicians but by technical aspects that are usually well-defined.

For instance, Bitcoins use a cryptographic algorithm to generate the currency made by the network users and not by a centralized governing body. To open a conventional bank account, one might require several documents, such as a valid ID and proof of residence, whereas to register to a digital currency, a valid IP address will suffice. Individuals can use Bitcoins to pay others or merchants without involving any third party.

When someone talks about Bitcoin or the Bitcoin blockchain, they usually refer to the Bitcoin network. There are two types of participants in the network: wallets and nodes. Bitcoin users typically own wallets with which they can send and receive payments. Nodes are the computers that validate and verify Bitcoin transactions and maintain a history of past transactions. A node can also act as a wallet. Some Bitcoin network nodes are also known as miners.

Anyone can join the network as a wallet or node and then start using Bitcoin on equal terms with everyone else. Although every network participant can send and receive Bitcoin payments, only nodes keep track of the entire history of transactions in real time. This log of transactions maintained by the nodes is called the Bitcoin blockchain. The word blockchain comes from adding new transactions in segments called blocks. 

A block is a permanent record of recent transactions. The blocks of recorded data build upon each other to form the blockchain, dating back to the first Bitcoin transaction. The transparency established by the blockchain is essential in securing the validation process as it allows the community to monitor and self-police transaction activity. It also allows for verification of both the spender and the recipient and ensures that double-spending a Bitcoin is impossible. In the case of Bitcoin, new blocks are added roughly once every 10 minutes. Nodes validate every new transaction and ensure that only legitimate transactions get added to the blockchain.

A node can operate autonomously and requires no maintenance; it only requires an internet connection. Thus anyone can set up a node without much effort. Nodes communicate with each other in a peer-to-peer fashion. Peer-to-peer is like a network where each node relays information to a few other nodes, and eventually, the message gets to all the nodes in the network. If a node tries to cheat the network by marking an invalid transaction as valid, it would be rejected by all other nodes on the web. Therefore, nodes collectively guarantee the network's transparency, availability, decentralization, and security. The nodes are spread worldwide, and with over 10,000 Bitcoin nodes powering the network at any given time, cheating on such a network is nearly impossible.

# How Do I Get Bitcoins?

Obtaining Bitcoins and learning to use the blockchain network and ecosystem is relatively simple. The three fundamental ways to get Bitcoins include

Transactions: Individuals and businesses can opt to receive Bitcoins for goods and services rendered or for peer-to-peer payments.

Digital Currency Exchanges: A digital currency exchange operates similarly to traditional fiat currency exchanges, allowing individuals to buy and sell Bitcoins at the current market rate. The exchange rate of Bitcoins varies based on liquidity, demand, and various other market conditions. 

Mining: Miners are the backbone of the Bitcoin ecosystem. They receive monetary rewards in Bitcoin for the services they provide for the network. This includes solving the algorithms necessary to process transactions, which helps secure the network and keep it running smoothly.

# How Do I Store Bitcoins and Make Transactions? 

Individuals must set up a digital wallet to safely store and transact Bitcoins. A wallet is a file that holds all the unique keys (passwords) attributed to an owner's Bitcoins. These keys will be recorded and synchronized when the system sends or receives payments. Wallets usually come in two forms: hot and cold. Hot wallets allow instantaneous transfers online through the Blockchain; they are connected to the internet. Cold wallets are offline wallets on a user's computer or device. Cold wallets offer greater security as they are offline, but the transaction process is longer and more cumbersome than a hot wallet. 

To receive Bitcoins, you provide the sender with your wallet address. A wallet address is a long sequence of numbers and letters. It is the unique identifier used to send payments to you. New addresses can be generated for each transaction to maintain greater financial privacy. 

Bitcoin technology significantly improves over traditional payment systems when utilizing the currency for both micro and large-scale payments. Micropayments can be made in increments as small as a fraction of a penny (0.00000001). The lowest possible unit of digital money, known as Bitcoin, is called a satoshi. It takes its name from Satoshi Nakamoto, credited with creating Bitcoin. The exchange rate for satoshi to bitcoin is currently set at 100 million satoshis to one bitcoin. For example, if you bought a $100 item with one bitcoin, your charge might ring up as .00341583 BTC (if BTC equaled $29,275.50). However, it's easier to understand 341,583 Satoshi. Conversely, the system can make large-scale payments without worrying about daily transaction limits or slow deposit processes standard at many traditional financial institutions.

# Lifetime Price Analysis of Bitcoin

In its decade-long rise, Bitcoin has changed how global markets work and made many people super-rich. The world's most popular cryptocurrency started on January 3, 2009, by mining the genesis block (Block 0), which created 50 Bitcoins. On January 9, Version 0.1 of Bitcoin was officially released, including the generation system that limits Bitcoin's total circulation to 21 million by 2040.

We now walk through Bitcoin's eventful journey to the top and analyze some of the significant milestones hit by the coin along the way.

2009 - 2011

After its inception in January 2009, Bitcoin remained more or less confined to the developers' circles and didn't catch much attention outside of it. But as October of 2009 came around, A pro-Crypto website named New Liberty Standard, which an original Bitcoin developer ran, displayed the first BTC-USD exchange rate. At the time, they calculated the exchange rate based on the cost of electricity required to mine one Bitcoin, which was around $0.0007 on October 5, 2009. Less than a month later, Bitcoin 0.2 was released with Linux support and new features that upgraded the blockchain capabilities. In May of 2010, the first Bitcoin purchase occurred when Programmer Laszlo Hanyecz bought two Papa John's pizzas for 10,000 Bitcoins (worth about $30) through an exchange on the Bitcointalk chat forum. By late 2010, Bitcoins popularity was increasing beyond the original media, and it was gaining momentum; this led to Bitcoins total market cap hitting the $1 million mark by November 2010. One Bitcoin was worth about $0.5 at this point.

2011 was a significant year for Bitcoin as it breached the $1 mark for the first time and made global headlines, further boosting its popularity. The infamous Silk Road website was also launched around this time by Mr. Ross Ulbricht, who wanted to create a lawless dark web marketplace where people could buy and sell whatever they wanted without a trace. Bitcoin is an obvious choice of currency as it is anonymous and untraceable. However, with the momentum gained from media attention, Bitcoin continued to rise, going beyond $10 to over $30 by June 2011. As this happened, the original creator, Satoshi Nakamoto, disappeared from the internet.

After rallying from $1 in February to highs of $31 on June 8, the dream run of Bitcoin suddenly reversed, the Bitcoin bubble as it was known - burst -and prices tumbled back down to single digits by June 12.

The sudden climb gave crypto fans massive hope in the new digital currency, with some expecting the price to reach highs beyond $1,000 and enter mainstream usage by the end of the year. But the sudden crash brought as much despair as the climb had given hope, and soon the existence of black market applications like Silk Road had people questioning the future of Bitcoin. Bitcoin version 0.5 was in operation around this time.

2012-2017

Bitcoin, being ever-volatile, had been slowly rising again over the past few months after the price peaked back in June 2011, climbing back above $9 for the first time in almost a year. Following this, multiple events of Bitcoin exchanges getting hacked and Bitcoins stolen were reported. Bitcoin continued its bull run, breaking the all-important $100 barrier for the first time, jumping from $96.15 on March 31, 2012, to close at $104.70 the next day.

However, the price was over 70% in two days as a panic sell-off ensued, falling from $228.94 to $68.09 in just six days, leaving the currency back where it had started for the month. The secret online black market called Silk Road that uses Bitcoin for shady transactions was seized by the FBI in October 2013, and Bitcoin prices plunged by over 18% from $127.30 to $103.85 as a result, but the price rebounded the very next day. By November, Bitcoin surged almost ten times in value reaching $1000. China's foreign minister Wang Yi takes a positive stand and announces that people in China are "free to participate in the Bitcoin market" and promises to "adopt a long-term perspective" on the currency, bringing much optimism to the market participants.

By 2015, Bitcoin was now accepted by over 100,000 retailers worldwide: including Microsoft, Dell, Wikipedia, Twitch, Greenpeace, Expedia, and PayPal. The widespread merchant adoption reassured people that Bitcoin has real value as a currency, not just as a digital investment. By late 2017, Bitcoin was trading around the $1000 mark as stray hacking incidents continued.

In a gigantic jump, Bitcoin went from under $1,000 on January 1, 2017, to $10,840 on December 1, making many people very rich. It went on to top a record-breaking high of $20,089 and closed the day at an all-time high of $19,783.06.

2018-2020

Prices sank 3.60% after Twitter announced a ban on cryptocurrency advertisements joining the likes of tech giants Google and Facebook to protect investors from fraud. 

The crackdowns came as global regulators started to notice the potential risks in Initial Coin Offerings (ICOs) and other misuse and frauds related to cryptocurrency use. The price dropped from $8,518.64 to $7,471.18 during the week. The seventeenth generation of Bitcoin's original software client, Bitcoin Core 0.17.0, became available with significant upgrades by 2018 as prices kept diminishing. As 2019 came around, the price climbed above $10k, hitting $13,880 on June 26 before sliding back.

The plague struck in 2020, Covid-19 crashed into our lives, and markets worldwide plunged. Bitcoin doesn't escape either, falling by over 50% in less than a week as the world moves toward a total lockdown. On March 12, Bitcoin dropped from almost $8,000 to below $5,000, losing 39% daily. Market liquidity froze up, and investors fled into cash and commodity segments. Amazingly, however, the price was back up to pre-pandemic levels by May as Bitcoin traders got back into the game quickly.

By November 2020, Bitcoin was up 100% on the year at $15,770; the bullish momentum continued, with prices increasing to a daily high of $19,864 on November 30 before closing the month at $19,700. Soon enough, on December 16, the cryptocurrency blows past the $20,000 barrier before charging on up to reach a record-breaking high of $21,576 – a yearly gain of over 180%. The currency kept climbing, passing $24,000 on December 21 and $26,000 on December 26.

2021-present

The bull run is sustained as Bitcoin bowls into a record-breaking 2021 by smashing through the $30,000 barrier on January 2 and smashing past $40,000 less than a week later before reaching a high of $42,000 on January 8, resulting in a 40% gain in the first week of the year. It's been very volatile since Bitcoin topped $40,000 at the start of January, and the currency has displayed immense volatility.

It's lost a quarter of its value since then, dipping briefly below $30,000 on January 22 and again on January 27. In yet another example of the massive influence Elon Musk can wield online, he adds the #Bitcoin hashtag to his Twitter profile, and the price skyrockets, gaining almost 11% in a single day and continuing with a further 10% rise the following day.

Following this on Monday, January 8, Tesla announced in an SEC filing that it had invested $1.5 billion into Bitcoin as part of a new investment policy, sending prices skyward. It's been an excellent year for Bitcoin, and by February 2021, the price bounced through $50,000 for the first time. In a significant milestone, Bitcoin's price reflected 9% on February 19- taking its total market cap past $1 trillion for the first time, climbing above $53,000. Shortly after, Elon Musk announced over Twitter that after buying $1.5 billion worth of Bitcoin last month, Tesla will now accept Bitcoins as payment for its cars - integrating the cryptocurrency further into the financial system.

Bitcoin prices shot up 6.24% to a new high of over $63,000 in anticipation of Coinbase IPO. But soon, the crypto gets crushed under the weight of a reversal by Tesla to cease accepting payment in Bitcoin because of the environmental impact. Bitcoin inks down 13%. Things got worse as Bitcoin went through another lousy patch as China banned financial institutions from offering any crypto-related services, sending prices down over 14%.

Bitcoin's price, which started at around $60,000 in May, dropped to about $30,000; almost every other cryptocurrency also took a nosedive. Bitcoin was still in freefall after ending the week down 25% due to global regulatory obstacles. By May, Bitcoin was trading at $38,400. News from El Salvador sent prices galloping up as it could make the legal currency tender and embrace the digital token in an unexpected move that could dramatically shift the currents of the international crypto marketplace.

At Bitcoin 2021, El Salvador President Nayib Bukele announced that his country was officially adopting Bitcoin as a legal tender. Once implemented, it would be the first country in the world to do so and mark a significant milestone in the history of international monetary policy. It would also mean no capital gains tax for Bitcoin in El Salvador. Meanwhile, as China intensified its crypto mining crackdown and banned most mining operations, Bitcoin plummeted to its lowest price since January.

After a year of record highs and parabolic gains, crypto markets are going through a turbulent time that doesn't look to be ending any time soon. Bitcoin looks to be making a comeback, with prices popping to nearly $43k in August 2021 as Germany moves towards greater crypto adoption, allowing institutional funds to hold up to 20% in crypto assets for the first time.

Inflation, the energy crisis, and geopolitical upheaval owing to Russia's aggressiveness toward Ukraine lowered BTC and other top cryptocurrencies' values in 2022. BTC dropped below $30,000 for the first time since July 2021 in May 2022. This period sees the cryptocurrency markets decrease steadily and sometimes sharply. Stablecoins like TerraUSD and Terra Luna's failure contributed to the crypto price drop. Bitcoin traded around $20,700 as cryptocurrency hedge fund Three Arrows Capital defaulted on a bitcoin loan and was ordered into liquidation by a court in the British Virgin Islands. The FTX exchange went bankrupt after its CEO, Sam Blankman Fried, was accused of misappropriating funds. FTX's financial difficulties and possible fraud prevented Binance from bailing it out 2022 ended, Bitcoin's price dropped below $20,000, indicating the cryptocurrency market's health.

# Bitcoin vs. Banks

Bitcoin was created to decentralize the financial system. In other terms, it was designed to replace the centralized banking system. To date, it has not officially been able to capture the banks, but the efforts are still intact. So, the dominance of Bitcoin over banks would decentralize the banking system. Thus, we need to concentrate on the characteristics and features of decentralized and centralized strategies to understand if Bitcoin can take over the current banking system.

Before the introduction of Bitcoin in the market, we were exposed only to the working of the traditional banking system. Bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies work by storing the transactions on a decentralized public ledger. The ledger uses cryptographic algorithms to ensure their records are on point and the user's identity is encrypted.

Bitcoin owners have digital wallets where they can store their coins. And the work of the ledger is to ensure that a proper spendable balance is maintained in the wallet. Moreover, it tracks the transaction history to ensure the owner spends from their wallets.

Unlike traditional currencies, Bitcoin is not created by printing notes and coins. But are made through a process called ‘cryptocurrency mining.’ Mining solves complex mathematical puzzles to confirm and add transactions to the ledger (block). Now that you know the basic working, we can understand its implications on banks.

# Implications for Banks

In recent years, a drastic change has occurred in how people look at businesses and make transactions. One can exchange currency outside traditional banks with a few clicks on a mobile phone.

The significant point is that people need not physically go to a traditional bank if they require financing. With the development of the peer-to-peer network, as in the case of Bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies, it is getting much more popular and familiar, and people are turning away from banks and looking forward to more decentralized systems like Bitcoin.

# Bitcoin - A Threat to Traditional Banks?

In short, yes. Cryptocurrency enthusiasts have long ago identified that Bitcoin and its technology threaten the banking industry. Not just them, but banks too have strong perceptions on the replacement of working of banks with blockchain technology. For instance, BNP Paribas, a French banking giant, released a report regarding their discussion on the technology behind Bitcoin and how it could make traditional banks redundant.

Furthermore, an analyst for the bank compared the software behind Bitcoin with an invention like the steam and combustion engine and stated that it has the potential to transform the world of finance. A UK banking report concluded that Bitcoin and the following cryptocurrencies are a menace to traditional banks, especially if they need to consider people's new preferences and behaviors regarding how they wish to transact with money.

# The After Effects!

To understand Bitcoin's power, let us assume a scenario where Bitcoin has taken over the financial system. Here are the effects banks can have with the entry of Bitcoin in the world of finance.

# The Demise of the Banking System

Banks cannot create reserves and perform fractional reserve lending with Bitcoin. There is no means through which it can control Bitcoin, as Bitcoin has no representative and is fully decentralized. Banks can no longer inflate or deflate away the purchasing power of users’ saving accounts. Banks cannot do bailouts with Bitcoin if cryptocurrencies get adopted worldwide.

Banks cannot generate revenue from remittance businesses, which levy 10-30 percent in cross-border transactions.

Banks act as an intermediary for every transaction that is made. But, with Bitcoin in the market, banks can become inexistent as one of the main features of Bitcoin is the absence of middlemen. These possibilities could emerge if Bitcoin came into the mainstream business. And thus, banks consider Bitcoin as a potential threat to themselves. Moreover, some believe that Bitcoin’s impact on banks and the current technology will be beyond one’s imagination. And the financial elite who control the politicians and the media will do everything they can to stop that from happening.

# Advantages of Bitcoin Over the Traditional Banking System

Bitcoin, a decentralized system, has several benefits over the centralized system like banks, mainly because of the capability to operate without any intermediary and no case of failure at any point.

# Processing of Transactions

Local transactions in the banking system are settled quickly. However, when it comes to international fund transfers, banks take several days to process the transaction. On the other hand, Bitcoin, a peer-to-peer payment system, can process over $1 billion worth of transactions on average. Thus, transactions are processed within a few minutes, irrespective of the sender and receiver location. Banks work for five business days; typically, no transactions are settled during the weekend or holidays. Meanwhile, Bitcoin is open daily and has no concept of holidays and weekends.

# Offshore Banking

Financial institutions like JP Morgan, which dominates the offshore banking industry, have links with large banks, making it possible to clear billions of money very efficiently and securely. However, a downside is that the transfer of billions of dollars requires considerable manual labor, including transaction verification, Anti-money Laundering checks, and payment clearing. But being completely decentralized, Bitcoin does not require any manual work, as mentioned. Moreover, settlement and offshore banking are provided by Bitcoin with significantly lesser costs and a much more robust infrastructure.

Can Bitcoin take over the Banking system? Yes and no. With the incredible benefits Bitcoin offers, there is a possibility of Bitcoin ruling over the banking system. Since the traditional banking system is mature, people rely upon and believe in this system. At the same time, Bitcoin does have downsides in terms of volatility and regulation. Therefore, it cannot be said with certainty that Bitcoin will replace the existing centralized system, even though it can do so.

# Bitcoin’s Fundamental & Technical Analysis

Fundamental analysis determines an asset's value by looking at its financial performance, prospects, market value, and use cases before investing. We will now look at the fundamental analysis of Bitcoin by studying essential metrics such as market cap, liquidity, and the Bitcoin whitepaper, among other things.

Market capitalization, commonly called a market cap, is the total market value of an asset. Market capitalization is equal to the current market price of the currency multiplied by the number of coins in circulation. The market cap of Bitcoin tells us about its proliferation in the market and the extent of its adoption. It can also be used to predict future growth potential. A coin with a low market cap is likelier to see high growth than one with a very high market capitalization, whereas a coin with a high market cap tends to be more established and stable.

Bitcoin has the largest market cap of all cryptocurrencies. It is a well-established coin and comparatively a safe investment bet. The prominent market cap reflects the trust market participants have in the currency.

Liquidity measures how easily an asset can be bought or sold. Good liquidity is a sign of consistent supply and demand. Liquidity is also directly linked to trading volumes. High liquidity ensures an asset can be quickly and easily traded and exchanged relatively.

Analyzing Bitcoin's liquidity shows us an average daily trading volume of 2 - 4 billion dollars, meaning Bitcoin is a very liquid asset representing buyers and sellers readily available. The high trading volumes also indicate the market interest in the coin.

The nature of a coin's supply is a fundamental characteristic of a cryptocurrency. Bitcoin has a fixed collection of 21 million coins; the mining rate becomes half every four years. This is done to preserve the coin's value and counter inflation. A limited supply is almost always advantageous to a currency as it ensures rarity and counters inflation. This makes Bitcoin superior to many other assets due to the limited supply that gives it anti-inflationary properties. Meaning a Bitcoin investment can counter inflation.

Information such as the rate and number of transactions happening in the Bitcoin blockchain gives us insights into the activity taking place in the network. Transaction value can be used to understand the movement of money in a network as it shows how much matter was transferred in a time. Bitcoin has a very high transaction count and weight, as it is the top cryptocurrency traded currently.

Blockchains run on different algorithms, and the hash rate can be used to assess the health of a blockchain network. Bitcoin's healthy hash rate indicates a high interest surrounding the currency or increased mining rates. In simple terms, the hash rate can be said as the rate of mining of a coin. In combination with other metrics, the hash rate can be used to predict trends in a currency. 

Active addresses are the blockchain addresses that remain active in a given period. Methods of calculating this metric vary, but it is also a good indicator of the dynamic market participants in the Bitcoin ecosystem. Several unique addresses can also be studied to see how the addition of new users is progressing in the network. Bitcoin has more than half a million addresses active at any given time, hinting at its popularity and rate of adoption.

The whitepaper is a technical document that gives us an overview of the cryptocurrency project. Reading a project's whitepaper before investing in it is a good idea. A good whitepaper should define the network's objectives and give us an insight into the technology used, use cases, and the roadmap ahead of the coin. Bitcoin has solid and well-defined documentation backing its blockchain technology; only minimal modifications and additions were made from the original paper published by Satoshi Nakamoto in 2009.

A cryptocurrency project is only valuable as long as it stays relevant. It should always have different or well-established applications or some unique, irreplaceable aspect that keeps it appropriate in the crypto universe. Hence while studying crypto, one must pay close attention to the network's integration into real-world use cases. Bitcoin is the most widespread and proliferative. Cryptocurrency enjoys an almost monopoly over transaction volumes and has become a standard index currency in crypto, signifying its relevance.

Using fundamental analysis, Bitcoin traders can understand the cryptocurrency's value and decide whether it is an excellent time to buy or sell. However, relying too much on fundamental analysis without also using technical analysis can prevent a trader from buying or selling at the wrong time.

To limit this risk, traders usually combine fundamental analysis with technical analysis. For instance, a fundamental analyst might look at several demand indicators, conclude that Bitcoin is underbought, and then implement a technical analysis by reading charts to find the optimal entry point.

Alternatively, a trader might use technical indicators to determine an excellent time to sell and then use fundamental analysis techniques to confirm this view by looking at key demand drivers.

# Technical Analysis of Bitcoin

Technical analysis is an entirely different ballgame from fundamental analysis. In technical analysis, we take a statistical approach to study an asset by analyzing key metrics and studying charts using mathematical tools and functions. Here we will briefly discuss some essential methods and concepts that fall under technical analysis.

# Support and Resistance

A support level is a price level where the desire to buy is so strong that it can withstand the pressure of sellers. As a result, the fall is suspended, and prices start increasing again. Usually, a support level can be determined in advance, according to the trends established by the previous decline. Likewise, a resistance level is opposite to a support level and represents a level above the market, where sellers' pressure exceeds buyers' influence. The prices tend to increase if the asset takes support at a support level, but it is likely to decline further if it breaks the support level and moves downwards. Similarly, if an asset breaks a resistance level, it is expected to keep moving up but is likely to move down if it does not cross the level.

# Candlestick Charts

Unlike the traditional linear chart that shows the current and historical price data, Japanese candlesticks and bars use as many as four data points for a given time instead of one: 

1.  The opening price when the time began 
    
2.  The highest point the price reached during the time 
    
3.  The lowest point the price reached in the period 
    
4.  The closing price when the period ended 
    

These four points increase the information value of the chart, which means they can immediately indicate what is happening in the market. This is why almost always candlestick charts are preferred over regular line charts.

# Indicator-Based Trading

Technical indicators are a great way to predict future movement and make better trading decisions; here, we will discuss a couple of technical indicators that traders can use to predict the future moment of Bitcoins market price.

Simple Moving Average (SMA) is the most common trend indicator. It gives an average price of a traded instrument over a particular time and identifies the primary trend. It looks like a smooth line on the chart. Moving averages are of 3 types generally - SMA, EMA, and WMA. All the moving averages need to catch up because we average the cost. If the price on your chart goes up or down sharply, this indicator will react, but you will receive this signal with a delay. 

When the price exceeds the moving average, it's a buy signal. When it crosses below, it's a sell signal. So we buy when the closing price is above MA and sell when the closing price is below MA.

Relative strength index (RSI) is a widely used technical indicator. It was developed by American engineer Welles Wilder in 1978. RSI shows how much the price changes in the direction of its movement. It transforms the cost into a percentage, indicating overbought zones (below 30%) and oversold zones (above 70%). 

Thus, RSI values of 30% or below indicate an oversold condition, while RSI values of 70% or above indicate that security is becoming overbought. When the price from the overbought zone enters the neutral zone, it is a sell signal; when the cost from the oversold zone enters the neutral zone, it's a buy signal.

Although technical indicators are developed using robust methods of math and statistics, they are not 100% accurate all of the time. Experienced traders use more than one technical indicator and other crucial inputs such as market sentiment, financial news, and fundamental analysis techniques before trading. In addition to diverse information, seasoned traders have sound risk and money management strategies to ensure optimal outcomes.

# Why is Risk Management Crucial?

Trading is a game of probabilities. In other words, a trading market is a place that is never certain. Thus, it comes with default risk. As a trader, the primary should be to reduce and manage risk in the best way possible. Professional traders believe understanding risk management is more crucial than having a good strategy.

Risk refers to the probability of an event happening against the intended outcome. Trading involves making predictions between two choices. And risk in this domain is simply the percentage of the money you are ready to lose. For instance, if a trader's account balance is $1000 and says he is risking 2% on a trade, the trader is betting 2% of his account balance, which is $20. So, if the business goes against the trader, he would be at most $20.

# Risk Management Techniques

A trader who can manage their risk effectively can automatically maximize their profits. A professional trader’s approach is to control their losses rather than running after profits. Here are some risk management strategies that every trader should know mandatorily.

# 1\. Stop Loss and Take Profit

The first and foremost rule in trading is to have a stop loss and take profit for every trade. Stop-loss is an executable order that cuts the positions at a specified price when the market goes in the intended direction. On the other hand, take profit is also an executable order that liquidates the parts when the market reaches a specified price in the trader's favor. A stop-loss will keep a trader safe from further losses, and taking profit will lock in the money even if the market goes against the desired direction later.

# 2\. Position Sizing

Position sizing is the number of units of security invested by a trader. This is not a random number derived using the account size and risk tolerance.

Firstly, account size is the account balance in a trading account. For example, if a trader deposits $1,000 into his trading account, the account size will be $1,000.

Account risk is the percentage of the account size a trader is ready to lose on a trade. For example, if you set your account risk to 1%, you will risk 1% of your account balance on the work.

Trade risk is simply the stop loss for a trade. It is the difference between the entry price and the stop-loss price. For example, if the entry price for a buy is $210, and the stop loss is $160, the trade risk would be $50 per unit traded.

# Position size

Using the above two parameters, the position size for a trade can be determined. Mathematically, the position size is obtained by dividing the account risk by the trade risk. For example, if the account risk on a trade is $100 and the trade risk is $2 per unit, the position size would be $100/$2 = 50 units.

# 3\. Risk/Reward Ratio

Another critical risk management tool is the risk/reward ratio. This ratio compares actual risk to the potential gains in a trade. The risk/reward tells you whether a business is worth taking. For example, there could be situations when your stop loss has to be higher than the take profit. In such trades, you would be risking more than gaining.

Thus, this ratio is used to stay cautious with such scenarios. For instance, if a trader is risking $100 on a trade and the risk: reward is 1:1, he is betting $100 to make $100. Similarly, if the ratio were 1:2, he would risk $100 to make $200. Therefore, you should refrain from trading under 1:1 to safeguard your capital.

# Future of Bitcoin

The value of Bitcoin soared by more than 500% in 2020-21 at its peak. One Bitcoin is worth twenty times as much as an ounce of gold. For example, some governments like the Central African Republic and El Salvador have recently legalized Bitcoin as a payment method, increasing the usage of Bitcoin bought with current currencies such as the dollar. This meteoric rise in the value of this cryptocurrency has generated substantial public interest and an increase in its perceived value. It has also led many speculators and gamblers to rush to invest in this currency to achieve future exponential returns. Again, this has played the role of a significant catalyst in the rise of its perceived value and made the currency extremely volatile. The unique features of keeping the digital footprint of those who transact in Bitcoin anonymous and not being controlled by any centralized organization have made some traders perceive Bitcoins as an attractive investment asset. Bitcoin's finite supply concept has also made some traders compare it to traditional assets such as gold and silver. However, the constantly fluctuating value of Bitcoins also makes its valuation extremely volatile.

The lack of centralized control or regulatory body makes it prone to changing compliance issues and vulnerable to money laundering. These aspects have also made many people wonder whether Bitcoin is a bubble that will eventually burst. 

Bitcoin has strength by design to make it a viable currency that has elevated its status over the years, more notably its fixed supply. Bitcoin will be mined with diminishing returns every four years until the maximum number of Bitcoins is reached: 21 million. This aspect of Bitcoin is essential for its value. Due to the limited amount of Bitcoins, there will never be an overabundance of Bitcoin. This makes Bitcoin a good asset for investors to put their wealth into, as it generally does not lose value due to inflation. However, as with most cryptocurrencies, the price can fluctuate wildly based on many other external factors.

Cryptocurrency is uniquely positioned as a forerunner in transforming technology to long-standing financial systems. It can fill gaps in current financial technologies by its very nature. Bitcoin's technology allows individuals to exchange currency without needing a trusted third party, like a bank, to oversee the transaction. All that is required to use Bitcoin is a mobile phone, which many people can access. Due to Bitcoin's peer-to-peer networking capability, users can trade Bitcoin with each other by scanning QR codes displayed on their wallet application. This is a genuinely innovative solution to a problem that has existed for many years. The transactions will invariably increase as the user base grows, so the demand for a better cryptocurrency network and applications will come to the forefront.

There is a large, untapped market for developers to create these applications, as this technology could be adopted by any industry that relies on a third-party clearing system. Bitcoin's progression into becoming a transformative technology lies in its ability to solve long-standing problems in combination with an active and growing community of users and developers. Businesses are also beginning to see the benefits of using cryptocurrencies for international transactions. Using conventional methods, the trade can be hit with any unexplained fees as it crosses borders, making sending the correct amount to another business complex. Cryptocurrency has a significant advantage over traditional currencies thanks to its agility in fast peer-to-peer transactions, especially in international business-to-business scenarios.

Internet marketplaces have been thriving and are genuine contenders to conventional brick-and-mortar stores. Amazon.com has grown to a degree that seems almost unexpected. Online shopping is flourishing, and Bitcoin is poised to extend its reach with efficient and easy payments for vendors and customers. One of Bitcoin's most significant opportunities is that it can act as a commodity, similar to gold. The value of gold can shoot up considerably whenever an event threatens the balance of global markets, as we have seen with the COVID-19 pandemic. The commodity market is a widely accepted and well-established form of trade, and cryptocurrency has seemingly begun to mimic the characteristics of gold. Gold has been a long-standing holder of value based on the universal acceptance and trust of its value. Cryptocurrencies could become a significant player in the commodity market. They have the unique property of being able to be purchased through a direct online mechanism, which creates an easy on-ramp for buyers. If Bitcoin remains a valid refuge for inflating fiat currencies, it will gain investors' confidence and move closer to becoming more mainstream.

It is well known that the "mining" of Bitcoins is a very energy-intensive process. Recent studies have shown that a single transaction in the Bitcoin network consumes 800 kilowatts of energy. By contrast, 500,000 Visa transactions can be carried out using the same point. In comparison to other payment methods, like cash or fiat money, Bitcoin is highly energy-intensive. Bitcoin's energy consumption is not unfavorable at its current price. Still, global warming and climate change have raised concerns over non-renewable energy sources used in Bitcoin mining. 

We are now experiencing the rise of Bitcoin, which may be the beginning of the next bull run in full steam in 2024.

# Expert Price Predictions

Bitcoin has stagnated over the latter half of 2021, falling by more than half from its peak of around $65,000 set in April.

However, the Bitcoin price is still significantly higher than when the massive rally began in October 2020, a bull run that sent the combined crypto market to a staggering $2.5 trillion before pulling back. Predicting the future of cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin is challenging, and the results could be more accurate to a high degree. Bitcoin being one of the most volatile assets out there, makes future valuations very difficult to get right. That being said, let's overview what cryptocurrency experts and financial analysts say about Bitcoin's future.

Tim Draper

Bitcoin bull Tim Draper had one of the most optimistic calls on Bitcoin, predicting the token would be worth $250,000 by the end of the year.

Shervin Pishevar

Shervin Pishevar, a venture capitalist and angel investor who co-founded Hyperloop One and Sherpa Capital, tweeted that Bitcoin will hit $100k. The prediction came soon after meeting with MicroStrategy CEO Michael Saylor, whose company invested more than $1 billion in Bitcoin.

Thomas Fitzpatrick

Citibank's global head of their market insights product, CitiFX Technicals, Thomas Fitzpatrick, made headlines for his Bitcoin valuation of $318,000, which came up after a report was leaked online. His analysis drew similarities between the gold market of the 1970s and Bitcoin's price action and pointed out the acceleration in money-printing by central banks since the emergence of COVID-19, which may fuel the Bitcoin run.

Winklevoss twins

Winklevoss twins – the famous Bitcoin billionaires, have come out and said Bitcoin has the potential to hit a price of $500,000 by 2030. This would put its market cap on par with gold at around $9 trillion.

Wences Casares

Wences Casares is the CEO and founder of Xapo, a Bitcoin wallet startup, and a board member at PayPal, among other roles. Casares said that Bitcoin will hit $1 million sometime before 2027 at the Consensus 2017 conference in New York.

Chamath Palihapitiya

Chamath Palihapitiya is the Founder of Social Capital and Co-Owner of the Golden State Warriors basketball team. He started his Bitcoin investment journey with an investment back in 2012, and by 2013 he had included Bitcoin in all of his portfolios. At one point, he owned 5% of all Bitcoin in circulation.

Palihapitiya has claimed that Bitcoin price will reach $100,000 in the next 3-4 years, adding that it will reach $1 million by 2037. He said, "This thing has the potential to be comparable to the value of gold…This is a fantastic hedge and store of value against autocratic regimes and banking infrastructure that we know is corrosive to how the world needs to work properly."

Jeremy Liew

Jeremy Liew is a partner at Lightspeed Venture Partners. He was one of the first investors in the social media app Snapchat. In an interview with Business Insider, Liew said that the Bitcoin price could "realistically" reach $500,000 by 2030. Following this, Liew's prediction was backed by Peter Smith, the CEO and co-founder of Blockchain — the world's most popular Bitcoin wallet.

Mark Yusko

Mark Yusko is a billionaire investor and Founder of Morgan Creek Capital. He predicts Bitcoin to be worth over $400,000 in the long term, a statement that made headlines because of its grandiose scale.

"It's just math. It is gold equivalent; there is about 20 million Bitcoin available today. Gold today is about $8 trillion. That gives you $400,000. And that doesn't include use cases related to currency," Yusko said. He further alluded to the fact that cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin strike fear into the hearts of bankers because Bitcoin altogether eliminates the need for banks.

Kay Van-Petersen

Kay Van-Petersen is an analyst at Saxo Bank, an investment bank specializing exclusively in online trading and investment.

According to a feature by CNBC News, Van-Petersen believes that Bitcoin will rise to $100,000 per unit by 2027. He successfully predicted Bitcoin's flow up to $2,000 by 2017. Although optimistic, his most recent estimate suggests that Bitcoin would account for 10% of the global foreign exchange market volume.

Andy Edstrom

Wealth manager for a California-based investment advisory firm called WESCAP, Andy Edstrom is an investor and an avid Twitter user, constantly tweeting about the topic and even having written a book titled "Why Buy Bitcoin."

In February 2020, Edstrom predicted that Bitcoin would reach an $8 trillion market cap by 2030. This would put the price of a single coin at around $400,000 with a market cap around that of gold. Edstrom believes Bitcoin could become the world's default currency.

Mike Novogratz

Mike Novogratz is a former hedge fund manager investing and veteran investor in Bitcoin and blockchain technology. His several past predictions have been both correct and incorrect. In March 2019, Novogratz indirectly made a prediction, claiming Bitcoin would "easily" surpass gold's market cap of $7.5 trillion within the next ten years. This puts his prediction very close to Edstrom's, both in terms of timeline and magnitude. As evident from these testimonials, most experts in the crypto and financial space are bullish on Bitcoin, especially in the long-term perspective.

# Chapter 4: The Altcoin Market

# What are Altcoins?

Since its inception in 2009, Bitcoin has become the first and most popular cryptocurrency globally. It has paved the way for creating many other alternative cryptocurrencies (or Altcoins) looking to outperform each other in a dynamic and fast-moving financial market. Bitcoin has also been the most popular currency for users of Dark Net markets due to its semi-anonymous qualities, lack of regulation, and effectiveness in laundering the profits from online contraband sales. However, users have criticized Bitcoin as lacking true anonymity and have been accused of providing a trail of evidence for law enforcement to trace payments via its blockchain due to its design limitations. This led to an increase in the development of alternative cryptocurrencies (Altcoins) in which anonymity and security are prioritized to make tracing payments and laundering of proceeds almost impossible. But all Altcoins are not geared towards anonymity; any cryptocurrency, not Bitcoin, is termed an Altcoin. 

With enhanced security features, an Altcoin can see its price increasing dramatically due to demand. Still, it may be the currency of choice for the wider majority. Each Altcoin project is unique and has its own target niche, objectives, and blockchain technologies it was built upon.

# Bitcoin vs. Altcoins

Altcoins work in different ways than Bitcoin does. For example, while Bitcoin produces only new Bitcoins every 10 minutes, altcoins such as Litecoin create new coins every 2.5 minutes. This means Litecoin can process payments faster. Moreover, Litecoin's total supply is capped at around 84 million Litecoin, whereas Bitcoin's total is about 21 million.

Litecoin also uses a different set of rules for mining than Bitcoin. Bitcoin mining requires costly hardware, whereas Litecoin can be mined with standard computer hardware.

Litecoin is just one of the thousands of altcoins available today. However, some altcoins stand out as popular alternatives to Bitcoin; although they don't reach Bitcoin's $100 billion market cap, most have some advantage over conventional Bitcoin. A few examples of such altcoins include:

*   Ethereum - ETH
    
*   Ripple – XRP
    
*   Cardano – ADA
    
*   Solona – SOL
    
*   Polygon – MATIC
    
*   Polkadot - DOT
    
*   Litecoin – LTC
    
*   Avalanche - AVAX
    

Generally, these altcoins were built and released to improve on Bitcoins flaws by providing faster transactions, faster mining rates, more security, or lower network fees.

However, these altcoins may need more stability, liquidity, and a large market cap that Bitcoin offers.

# Early Examples of Altcoins

One of the earliest notable Altcoins, Litecoin, released in October 2011, was marketed as the "silver to Bitcoin's gold." While fundamentally similar in code and functionality to Bitcoin, Litecoin diverges from Bitcoin in several ways. Litecoin allows transactions to be approved more frequently. It also provides for a total supply of 84 million coins to be created, which is four times Bitcoin's total supply. Litecoin is still among the most popular altcoins; some even think it could be a better investment than Bitcoin.

# Types of Altcoins

Depending on their functionalities and blockchain mechanisms, altcoins come in many varieties and categories. Here's a rundown of some of the more important ones:

# Mining-Based

As their name indicates, mining-based altcoins are generated through mining. Most altcoins that fall into this category use Proof-of-Work (PoW). Proof-of-work is how computers generate new coins by solving complex problems to create blocks. Examples of mining-based altcoins are Litecoin, Zcash, and Monero. Most of the top altcoins in early 2020 were mining-based coins. Pre-mined coins are the alternative to mining-based altcoins. Pre-mined coins are not produced through an algorithm but distributed before being listed in a cryptocurrency exchange. An example of a pre-mined coin is XRP, also known as Ripple.

# Stablecoins

Cryptocurrencies, since their launch, have been very volatile by nature. Stablecoins aim to reduce this overall volatility by clinching their value to various goods or commodities, such as precious metals, fiat currencies, or other cryptocurrencies. This ensures that investors can redeem their money if the cryptocurrency fails or faces problems. Price fluctuations for stablecoins don't usually go beyond a narrow range.

Social media giant Facebook's Diem is the most famous example of a stablecoin. It is a dollar-backed coin, meaning Diem's value is linked to the US dollar. Other examples of stablecoins include USDC and MakerDAO.

# Security Tokens

Security tokens are comparable to securities traded in stock markets, except they have a digital origin. Security tokens are similar to traditional stocks, and they typically promise equity in the form of ownership or a dividend payout to holders. The possibility of price appreciation for such tokens is a major draw for investors to buy and hold these coins. Security tokens are generally offered to investors through ICOs or initial coin offerings from major crypto exchanges.

# Utility Tokens

Utility tokens are used to provide and avail services within a network. For example, they might be used to redeem rewards or purchase services. Utility tokens, unlike security tokens, do not pay dividends. Filecoin, used to buy cloud storage space on a network, is an example of a utility token.

# Crypto Adoption Token (CAT)

The CAT Token was released in 2023 and introduced as a cutting-edge Pro-Crypto Public Education, Adoption, and Awareness Token, Including advertising campaigns to promote Crypto. Join them and others to spread the word about cryptocurrency's benefits and help revolutionize the world of finance. Tokens like this will invest in pro-crypto politicians and support regulatory measures through funding. They will secure a lobbying company and empower the next generation with strategic crypto education and Onboarding. The goal is to provide crypto content to the masses, including schools, libraries, independent seminars, workshops, and beyond.

CAT is revamping the crypto news arena for a seamless experience and introducing the goals to establish a fair and impartial news network and disseminate the news that will revolutionize the industry and surpass what existed before. Discover these high-quality alternatives to other subpar options.

# Are Altcoins Good Investments?

The market for altcoins is still very new. And in recent times, the number of altcoins listed in cryptocurrency markets has rapidly multiplied. It has attracted crowds of retail investors, relentlessly betting on their price movements to book quick profits. But retail investors need more capital to generate sufficient market liquidity.

Shallow markets and an absence of regulation produce immense volatility in altcoin markets and valuations.

There is money to be made buying and selling altcoins, but there's also money to be lost. Even though this is true with almost any investment, altcoins come with unique risks.

Altcoin prices are incredibly volatile, and most individual investors cannot manage this volatility. Plus, there needs to be more regulation in place around altcoins. Widespread fraud also happens in the cryptocurrency market when scammers lure investors into buying fake cryptocurrencies.

That doesn't mean you should refrain from buying altcoins altogether. Altcoins be a source of diversification for an investor's portfolio as each altcoin project is usually independent of the others.

Cryptocurrency markets have yet to mature, and there are no defined investment criteria or metrics to evaluate cryptocurrencies. The altcoin market is driven mainly by speculation. Several cases of death and failed cryptocurrencies exist.

Therefore, the altcoin market is for investors prepared to endure the risks of operating in an unregulated and emerging market prone to high volatility and wild price swings.

Looking Ahead

Altcoins improve on the original Bitcoin, aiming to fix the cryptocurrency's shortcomings. Altcoins, such as Ripple's XRP and Ethereum's ether, have already become popular in the mainstream financial world, resulting in high valuations for these coins. The SEC has sued some of these coins, claiming they are securities, making it hard to trade with USD. There is a reason the corrupt financial system does not want these to succeed.

Looking at the current state of the altcoin market, it is likely that all the altcoins will stay. But it is also expected that most of the more than 2000 altcoins listed in crypto markets will not survive. The altcoin market will coalesce around many altcoins with solid utility and use cases, dominating the markets.

Altcoins are an inexpensive way for investors in the crypto space to expand their horizons beyond Bitcoin. Rallies in Altcoin markets have produced returns multiples of those produced by Bitcoin. But there is significant risk involved in Altcoin investing, like the absence of regulation and immense price volatility. Before buying any altcoins, research and have sound risk management strategies. If trades are timed correctly, one can expect multi-bagger returns in the Altcoin markets.

# The Market Capitalization of Altcoins

The altcoin market has been booming in recent years; from 2017 to the present day, the total market cap of the altcoin space has increased by more than 100-fold. The altcoin market cap currently stands at 884 billion dollars, higher than Bitcoin's $665 billion. Let's look at some of the front runners in the altcoin space that account for a significant portion of this impressive market cap.

Ethereum ($221 billion)

Ethereum is a cryptocurrency that draws inspiration from the original Bitcoin. While Bitcoin is used primarily as a store of value as a digital form of currency, the idea behind Ethereum is to decentralize applications and services from simple apps to more complex financial agreements. Ethereum uses the same technology as Bitcoin, wherein a blockchain uses a distributed, decentralized public ledger to decentralize the network, so it's not under the control of any single entity.

Ethereum lets you send cryptocurrency to anyone for a small fee and powers applications everyone can use. Ethereum boasts itself as 'the world's programmable blockchain. It builds on Bitcoin's innovation, with some significant differences.

While both Ethereum and Bitcoin let you use digital money without payment providers or banks, Ethereum has the added advantage of being programmable, so you can also use it for many different use cases and business models. This also means Ethereum’s application can go beyond fulfilling payments on a network. It can be a marketplace of financial services, games, and apps that can't censor or steal your data. Ethereum was conceived by Vitalik Butarin, Martin Wood, and other members in 2013 and released after a couple of years in 2015. Through the Ethereum project, they wanted to address different limitations present in Bitcoin and allow the execution of short programs in the Blockchain. Vitalik and the rest of the team sought better ways to build decentralized finance systems with apps running on the Blockchain.

At the heart of the programmable aspect of Ethereum lies intelligent contracts and dApps (Decentralized apps). A Smart Contract is a short program that can be deployed and run on a Blockchain. It processes transactions sent to it by executing code and can also hold funds(Ether) on the blockchain ledger. These transactions can pass both funds and data.

Smart contracts can be built to perform all sorts of functions without a trusted third party or central authority. If you consider Smart Contracts as back-end code running in the Blockchain, Decentralized Applications are the front-end or UI. They represent the visible layer connecting users or other applications with the Smart Contracts running in the Blockchain. Eventually, entire decentralized autonomous organizations may exist in an ether economy. 

Ethereum was trading at $2,913 as of Aug 2021 and $1,913 as of June 2023. Exactly years ago, it was trading at $390; that's more than a five times increase in market price.

As Ethereum's rally accelerates, many in the crypto industry predict that Ethereum could overtake Bitcoin as the world's biggest cryptocurrency by value. With the latest update to its Blockchain, Ethereum is returning to a cryptocurrency bull market with fresh all-time highs on the horizon.  The price boost, pushing ether tokens toward $3,000, has caused the combined crypto market cap to climb to almost $1.7 trillion.

Tether ($83 billion)

Tether or USDT is a cryptocurrency pegged 1 to 1 with the US dollar. Tether is currently the most popular USD-pegged cryptocurrency in the world. According to its developers, the design of USDT is to be worth 1 USD, and the dollar will back each tether unit it issues into circulation in a one-to-one ratio. However, the price of USDT can be volatile, and it was seen trading at a>10% discount to USD in 2018.

Many consider Tether an optimal way for users to move money effectively without a centralized authority. Users can trade the currency actively and use it for international transactions in countries with capital regulations. The massive global payments market is a driving force for Tether's supply and demand movements.

Though the white paper states that one USDT should receive the backing of one USD, there are many questions about its legitimacy. The reserve level has been a mystery as rumors suggest that Tether's reserve is insufficient to back the amount of USDT. The last audit was in mid-2018 by a Washington law firm. The mistrust and speculations brought in a significant price movement in Oct 2018.

The credibility of the Tether has always raised some concerns, especially regarding whether Tether's reserve has sufficient reserves. Despite such speculation, Tether remains at the top, holding ten times more market share than other USD stablecoins. Given the uncertainty surrounding its backing with USD, it will be interesting to see if Tether can maintain such dominance in the future.

Binance Coin ($40 billion)

Binance Coin is the cryptocurrency issued by the Binance exchange, the largest cryptocurrency exchange in the world—binance coin trades with the BNB symbol.

It was initially based on the Ethereum network but is now the native currency of Binance's Blockchain, the Binance chain.

Binance uses one-fifth of its profits to repurchase BNB. They then permanently destroy this purchased currency every quarter in a process known as burning. This keeps inflation at bay and induces scarcity to drive up prices. The updated whitepaper states that BNB burns will now be based on its exchanges' trading volume.

Since Binance began burning BNB in Oct 2017, 10 token burns have occurred, with about 16.7 million BNB worth approximately $208 million burned as of January 2020.

Binance was initially intended as a utility token for discounted trading fees during its release in 2017. But now, its uses have expanded to various other applications, including payments for transaction fees, travel bookings, entertainment, online services, and financial services.

Binance has a market cap of more than $56 billion and ranks among the top cryptos in terms of market cap. Binance's story started with an Initial Coin Offering (ICO) and has grown into one of the largest global cryptocurrency exchanges in terms of volume traded and market share by web traffic. 

Cardano ($12 billion)

Charles Hoskinson founded Cardano in 2017, two years after he helped develop the Ethereum blockchain technology. He also co-founded Internet Online Hong Kong (IOHK), the company that built the Cardano blockchain.
