Cardano uses a timestamping methodology called Proof of Stake (PoS). PoS is the system by which the miners in the Cardano ecosystem validate and may block transactions. A stake is simply the number of coins (ADA coins) one holds, which also determines one's participation in creating new blocks.

Using knowledge gained from developing the Ethereum project, Hoskinson injected the idea of smart contracts into the Cardano project. Cardano sets itself apart in the crypto-verse in that it was conceived through peer-reviewed research. The research yielded unique features like unparalleled platform security, system sustainability, and a robust professional development community. 

According to the project's website, IOHK created the Cardano blockchain to "restore trust to global systems" by availing a new standard in inclusive and open-source technology. Therefore, "changemakers, innovators, and visionaries" can leverage the technology to create a society that is "more secure, transparent, and fair."

ADA is the digital currency facilitating transactions among Cardano network participants. ADA ranks among the top ten tokens in terms of market cap. It enables peer-to-peer transactions in a decentralized, secure environment free from intermediaries. For this reason, transactions over the Cardano blockchain are fast and inexpensive.

Cardano's cryptocurrency claims to have legitimate advantages over fiat currency. The Cardano Foundation adopted a limit of ADA's total supply at 45 billion coins. The idea is to avoid inflation and keep the coin's value in check. ADA is far better than a fiat currency in terms of transaction speed by peer-to-peer transactions. Fiat currencies require intermediaries like banks to enforce trust, leading to bottlenecks along the transaction chain. 

XRP ($30 billion)

Ripple is a cryptocurrency token equivalent to digital cash, purpose-built for payment networks and banks. XRP or Ripple is a native token on its Blockchain known as RippleNet. RippleNet is a payments system run by Ripple Labs and built on the XRP Ledger, decentralized and open-source blockchain technology. It boasts breakneck transaction speeds with an average transaction settlement speed of four seconds. Comparably, the transaction settlement time on the Bitcoin network is 3,600 seconds. 

One of the areas where Ripple had an immediate impact was cross-border payments. The developers created RippleNet to streamline transactions. Consumers and businesses often suffer the inconveniences of converting between currencies and losses arising from exchange rate differences when transacting in fiat currency. RippleNet uses the XRP token as an intermediary to streamline fiat currency exchange without suffering slippage. 

Moreover, digital currency facilitates faster settlement of transactions at speeds unseen in the traditional payments sectors. Due to the utilization of the peer-to-peer transaction system, users enjoy micro-fees where the average fee is less than $0.0003403. RippleNet's competitors in the conventional payments sector, such as VISA, charge thousands of times higher for an equivalent transaction. 

Ripple draws its strength from blockchain technology. Most blockchain platforms obtain consensus through Proof of Work (PoW) or Proof of Stake (PoS) mechanisms. The PoW system is slower and consumes more power, unlike the PoS system, which supports faster validation and approval of transactions while consuming less power.

However, blockchain platforms like XRP Ledger utilize another form of consensus, in this case, called the Ripple Protocol Consensus Algorithm (RPCA). Peace is how nodes in a blockchain network maintain agreement and correctness with each other. RPCA runs through the more than 150 nodes that make up the global XRP Community. 

The robust consensus system is a significant reason behind Ripple's seven years of stability, enabling its developers to expand the product range of the ecosystem. For example, the developers have expanded RippleNet's capacity to offer on-demand liquidity to its enterprise clients. This makes global payments faster and cheaper. 

# Promising Altcoins

Some altcoins have immense potential to dominate future markets. We will now look at such altcoins whose market cap has been gradually increasing over the years.

Basic Attention Token (BAT) is a utility token built on the Ethereum blockchain, which is the foundation for the Brave web browser platform. The Brave browser allows users to browse the internet anonymously and completely ad-free and then enables users to opt-in to watch ads in exchange for BAT tokens.

Publishers also get BAT when users watch or click on ads on their sites. Advertisers benefit from lower overhead costs for advertising and higher engagement from users. And users get increased privacy without sacrificing the benefits of tailored advertisements and can get BAT tokens. The BAT token is designed to be exchanged between users, advertisers, and publishers. It is based on the concept of user attention, which is critical to advertising companies operating in the digital age. BAT is an ERC-20 token secured by a rigorously tested proof-of-work consensus algorithm supported by an extensive Ethereum miner network. This algorithm ensures that only valid transactions are confirmed, while the combined work of the Ethereum mining network ensures BAT transactions are irreversible once approved.

The developers of BAT seek to improve how advertising is done in the digital space. They see the current system as disadvantageous for users, publishers, and advertisers. Users are relentlessly targeted with ads and trackers, and they are subjected to malware that is becoming increasingly aggressive and powerful.

Conventional publishers have seen their ad revenues decline dramatically as monopoly platforms like Google and Facebook drive down the price of information while taking a hefty cut from the advertising revenue. In addition, bots and malware can inflict increasing damage each year, and platforms have suffered credibility issues. Moreover, advertisers need more accurate targeting methods and information to deliver their content sufficiently because platforms monopolize these functions.

The creators of BAT believe that tracking how users interact with digital advertising content and storing that information in a distributed blockchain ledger will enhance and improve the digital advertising experience for advertisers, publishers, and users. Users will experience fewer ads and content that is better tailored to them, while Publishers will find new sources of revenue. And advertisers will be able to target particular users better based on documented user content preferences and interests. They will also be able to avoid fraud more efficiently.

Basic Attention Token has a maximum total supply capped at 1.5 billion tokens. Almost all of this supply is currently in circulation. Hence, the Basic Attention Token can be considered almost entirely liquidated.

In its 2017 ICO, 1 billion BAT tokens were sold to investors, the remaining 200 million coins were locked in a development pool, and 300 million BAT was reserved for the user growth pool (UGP). As of November 2020, the development pool and UGP pool addresses are almost empty. Basic Attention Token can be bought and sold on every central cryptocurrency exchange platform and has excellent liquidity.

Chainlink (LINK) is a cryptocurrency blockchain network that provides a link between real-world data and smart contracts. The platform helps execute smart contracts, which are blockchain agreements that automatically complete upon filling in necessary preconditions. It offers a tradable cryptocurrency token called LINK, used to pay for innovative contract transactions on the Chainlink network. LINK tokens also hold their value in comparison to fiat currencies.

Chainlink was created in 2014 and launched in 2017 by its founder Sergey Nazarov, a 32-year-old entrepreneur with a significant presence in the crypto market. Initially, the Chainlink network was designed to be a centralized oracle system that could verify incoming information. However, it has since transformed into a decentralized oracle network that pairs with smart contracts to secure transactions using external data sources and APIs.

Chainlink helps bridge the gap between oracles that send real-world data and smart contracts on the Blockchain. Chainlink can generate multiple subcontracts while taking data from only the most trustworthy nodes and oracles to maximize accuracy. Although there is no limit on the number of smart contracts the platform can handle, the number of LINK tokens that will ever exist is capped at one Billion. The Chainlink network is a reasonably open system. Anyone can join it, provide data to the network, or help operate its corresponding nodes and oracles. These operators are paid in LINK tokens, which are the native currency of the Chainlink network.

As the proliferation of blockchain technology is increasing daily, networks such as Chainlink can supplement innovative contract implementations, acting as alternatives to mainstream blockchains such as Ethereum.

Polkadot (DOT) is a network designed to 'join the dots with all other networks. Unlike networks like Bitcoin, which operate independently, Polkadot can link multiple networks.

Its ultimate aim is to act as a standard integrated framework for all blockchains that opt-in to use its technology. The idea is to take care of complex and costly cryptocurrency mining processes that include validation of transactions and security protocols and enable developers to focus solely on creating efficient dApps and smart contract functionality. Gavin Wood, a co-founder of Ethereum, built Polkadot.

Developers building innovative, decentralized systems must engineer these systems from the ground up, including the Blockchain. This means that time, talent, and resources are diverted into building competing networks rather than creating a standard everyone builds off. That's Polkadot's aim, to make way for developers to build value on top of all blockchains rather than just one.

Blockchains can connect with Polkadot and work in parallel as "Para chains" to access the network's proof-of-stake validation system for transactions and security. In the network's "relay chain," transaction addresses are checked, and data is standardized so that every participating system can understand it. 

While functionality is covered, blockchains connected to Polkadot can use their PoS mechanism to decide when and how to update their code and run the dApps or tokens they prefer. Polkadot is a pick-and-choose layer that allows networks to keep their security intact and incorporate new protocols simultaneously. The two fundamental issues blockchain-based systems must address are scalability—the number of transactions per second the network can handle—and governance: how the system manages protocol upgrades and changes. Polkadot aims to solve both of these problems efficiently.

DOT, the native token of the Polkadot network, allows holders to vote on potential code changes, which automatically upgrade across the network if a consensus is reached. Polkadot launched its main net in early 2020 and quickly gained traction among investors, developers, and cryptocurrency traders.

In less than a year, it has already racked up several impressive accolades and developments, including Climbing to become the fourth-largest cryptocurrency and achieving a peak market capitalization of almost $18 billion.

Undergoing redenomination, a process through which old DOT tokens are split 100 times to produce new DOT tokens—increasing the supply from 10 million to 1 billion and Seeing a dramatic surge in developer activity, with over 350 projects now being actively developed on the platform.

With blockchain technology penetrating conventional business models and use cases, we can expect more developers to get on board the smart contract and dApps universe; a framework model that Polkadot proposes will be of paramount importance in such a situation where developers are required to be more focused on creating robust smart contracts and dApps rather than devoting time to build a blockchain from the ground up.

# Future of the Altcoin Market

With the astronomical price of Bitcoin, Altcoins have become a viable investment option for new investors because these cryptocurrencies are reasonably priced; they are bought much more.

Most traders in the present-day cryptocurrency market are small retail investors who cannot buy and sell Bitcoin as they need to possess adequate capital, so they naturally trade.

Altcoins are generally cheaper than Bitcoins and are available to users at a better price. Moreover, these coins have better future growth potential when compared to Bitcoin. If proper research about a blockchain project and the potential for scalable growth can be identified, such altcoins can be a great investment opportunity that can produce multifold returns quickly. Ethereum is an example of such an altcoin that rewarded early investors that identified its potential.

Cryptocurrency represents the beginning of a new period of technology-driven markets that will eventually disrupt conventional market strategies, longstanding business methods, and traditional regulatory policies. Cryptocurrencies carry the ground-breaking potential to allow their consumers access to a global payment system in which participation is only a matter of internet availability or ownership of a crypto wallet rather than by factors such as having a bank account.

# Chapter 5: Decentralised Finance

# What is DeFi?

Decentralized finance (abbreviated as DeFi) has taken the world by a whirlwind since its inception in 2017. The basic constitution of this term indicates the involvement of finances and Decentralization. While "finances" is self-explanatory, Decentralization is a descriptive term requiring extra elaboration for comprehension. "Decentralization" here highlights that this system is free from the control of any chief body like banks; in other words, it is not centralized. It stands at the crossroads of financial services and technology.

DeFi has amalgamated the two sectors with such efficiency that the need for intermediaries like brokers or banks has been eliminated. Compared to the financial handling by mediators, DeFi does not require an account, government-issued ID, address proof, etc. In other words, DeFi allows vendors, lenders, borrowers, etc., to engage in monetary transactions much like a bank but without the actual involvement of a centralized authority like that of a bank. It is a much less cumbersome finance system that does not require the service seeker to go through extensive processes like generating bank statements, producing many documents, etc. 

The operation of these services is facilitated by blockchain technology. Blockchain is an immutable ledger that successfully stores, tracks, and records transactions in the simplest form. Such a system makes the exchange process of financial services between the seeker and receiver smoother using intelligent contracts embedded in the blockchain. A smart contract is a digital agreement between two or more parties with terms agreed upon by both and subsequently signed. A well-formulated smart contract promises security in terms of its information. Since the contract would be present in blockchain, the accessibility to the signed contract for malign purposes can be reduced, thus bolstering the security of the insurance. 

# How Does DeFi Work?

DeFi uses cryptocurrencies and smart contracts, driving out the need for interventions by brokers or other intermediaries. The functioning of DeFi can be understood by first understanding the essential components of DeFi. 

# Components of DeFi

Before understanding the features and characteristics of DeFi, it is crucial to understand the components that constitute DeFi. Due to the premise that DeFi is a financial ecosystem, it is only pertinent that it works akin to other economic ecosystems. Due to this, The broad categorization of components of DeFi includes:

# Stable Currencies (Stablecoins)

Additionally, for every financial service exchange on DeFi, smart contracts are used. Intelligent contracts are central to DeFi's functioning because they secure the terms and record the transaction activities to ensure the agreed-upon terms are met. Now, let's move on to understanding the components of the DeFi system in the context of a software stack.

The consolidation of multiple layers characterizes a software stack. These various layers, in turn, have components that have a definitive specific function to cater to in developing a DeFi system. The four layers comprise the

# Defi Software System Stack is:

The Settlement Layer is the first of the four layers constituting the DeFi software system. It can be comprehended as a base layer providing the platform for DeFi transactions. A settlement layer is paramount because it is the anchor of the financial ecosystem of DeFi. A public blockchain and cryptocurrency are their essential constituents for functioning. For example, Bitcoin and Ethereum are the quintessential representatives of the settlement layer.

Blockchain Protocol: These protocols are pertinent rules and regulations necessary to secure the reliable functioning of the system. Compliance with these rules allows operators in this industry to work unhindered. This layer ensures the conversion of the assets into cash in the DeFi ecosystem providing liquidity. 

Application Layer: This layer simplifies the protocol mentioned above to ensure interface services, making the system and its services available to consumers. For example, this layer can allow borrowing services to be disseminated here. While there are different categorizations of the various layers, these layers mentioned above have been compressed to ensure the readers' understanding and avoid ambiguity.

Having understood the essential components of the DeFi ecosystem, let's understand the actual working process of these systems. Since the DeFi works on blockchain networks only thus, its working process is quite similar to that of blockchains. The DeFi blockchain ensures the security of financial services and transactions by issuing users encrypted keys. These keys enable the users to receive a unique identifier they can only access. This system has two essential keys- private and public keys.

These dual keys cater to different functions. For example, a Public key is used by interested service seekers or peers to find the system's owner. The owner can use a private key to access and authorize any financial activity or transactions involving their funds. The private key is crucial as it acts as the owner's digital wallet through which the user can exchange cryptocurrencies. Only the owner can put the transaction into motion by authorizing the transaction with the private key. The transaction is set into action once the transaction request is verified by all the relevant parties involved. It is pertinent to mention that a unique ID accompanies every transaction to maintain high-security standards and prevent any malpractices on the platform. With an understanding of the working of DeFi, let's move on to see the features of this system.

# Key Features of DeFi

The central characterizing features of DeFi also fall in tandem with the benefits offered by this decentralized method of disseminating financial services across the globe. Most of these features are reinforced by DeFi services' Blockchain network. From defining features like decentralization and permission nature of the systems to implicated qualities like increased transparency, immutability, etc., DeFi constitutes numerous characteristics. Some of the major ones have been outlined below:

# The Premise of Decentralization

The first and foremost feature of DeFi, as its constituent term indicates, is Decentralization. As explained in the previous sections, Decentralization means no centralized authority or intermediary to add to the tediousness and intricacies of the basic financial services process. In the absence of any such intermediaries, the seekers of such financial services can access these services without any constraints placed upon them. This feature is elaborated upon in the next part, which is permissionless and closely related to Decentralization. 

# Permissionless Nature DeFi Systems 

'Decentralized finance' defines this feature, and including the word decentralized indicates the flagship feature being permissionless. Decentralization of financial services has allowed the seekers and receivers to interact on a platform that requires no oversight by an external centralized authority like a bank. It also reduces the dependency on banks and other intermediaries while seeking financial services easily accessible by DeFi platforms. This feature confers the advantage of being independent and user-friendly to the DeFi systems. So any individual can access these services with the minimal requisites of a crypto wallet and, of course, an internet connection. For example, Ethereum has its basis in a permissionless blockchain which has assisted the proliferation of this platform. Today, Ethereum is leading the DeFi industry.

The permissionless nature of DeFi's operation might even instigate doubts about security. The security of the operational activities on DeFi's systems is supplemented by intelligent contracts, which ensure that the terms and conditions of financial services are met, thus ensuring the security of the finances. Also, all the transactional activities are stored on the blockchain itself. 

# The Premium Level of Transparency

Against the general notion that Decentralization might decrease transparency, Decentralization offers openness to a great extent. Since the distributed ledger encompasses all the transactional activities on the blockchain network, it provides scope for solidified clarity. Transparency extends to financial data and activities and applies to smart contracts. Transparency in the case of intelligent contracts expands the content of the review of its code by anyone. It ensures the presence of information for each individual. 

Therefore, any individual might make an informed decision for availing of the financial services offered by the smart contract in focus based on its review. Furthermore, the context of transparency in the case of DeFi systems is also supported by the basic cryptographic regulations applying to the blockchain, which promise that the information is added only after proper verification and authentication procedures. The DeFi protocols existing on the Ethereum platform necessitate the openness of the source code for users to review. In the case of a public Ethereum chain, the information is broadcasted to all the users, bolstering the premise of transparency.

# Immutability

One of the significant advantages DeFi offers is the immutability of financial data by reducing the possibility of any malicious data tampering. Data storage on Blockchain networks makes the records extremely secure, reducing the case of any unauthorized change in the papers. This, in turn, decreases the prospect of fraudulent practices reigning in this financial system. Also, smart contracts on the blockchain make the terms and conditions unchangeable by a single party due to the availability of activity regarding the intelligent agreements to all the concerned parties. This distinguishing feature of DeFi ensures a highly secure space and dialogue for exchanging financial services.

# Accessibility

As mentioned in the previous section, anyone can use DeFi financial systems with minimum prerequisites of a crypto wallet and an internet connection. This allows users across the world to relish the advantages offered by DeFi. Although such ledgers are permissionless in their essence, they might create many problems, like the proliferation of fraudulent activities or perpetrators disrupting the financial ecosystem set up by DeFi. Due to the same, it is pertinent for service seekers and givers on DeFi platforms to authenticate the other party by checking the Know-Your-Customer (KYC) requisites and, sometimes, Anti-money laundering (AML). Due to relevant strategies to prevent malpractices, accessibility still weighs more towards the side of benefits conferred.

# Flexibility and Autonomy

The permissionless feature of DeFi systems offers untamed flexibility and autonomy to the users. Again, this is because there is no regulating body to hinder the progression and evolution of services and their scope of DeFi systems. The users are not required to display compliance with a defined set of rules and regulations forced on them by external authorities. The flexibility offered to the software, for example, in the case of smart contracts, leads to autonomy. Once the interested parties have entered into intelligent agreements with mutual alignment, smart contracts are self-functional, requiring nothing from the involved parties except complying with the terms stated. Thus they can easily carry out their business without needing external authority, augmenting autonomy.

Now that we know the characterizing features of the DeFi systems let's compare this system with the traditional financial methods and assess which system has more significant advantages. 

# Comparison Between DeFi And Traditional Financial Systems

The traditional financial systems include conventional banks and other financial institutions governed by a centralized authority, thus often used interchangeably with Centralized finance (CeFi). Centralized exchanges, part of the CeFi, jeopardize user funds, data security, and privacy as these centralized authorities manage both. However, these centralized exchanges allow various financial services like margin trading, borrowing, lending, etc. Also, CeFi effectively provides cross-chain services standing in strong contrast to DeFi. CeFi considers a multitude of chains for taking complete control of the funds, and only after that, it allows the trading of coins. Also, they have the feature of transfer of ownership that reinforces its simplicity in the case of cross-chain services. Additionally, CeFi systems also offer flexibility in working with fiat currency. Despite the advantages of CeFi or traditional financial methods, DeFi systems are gaining mainstream traction in the financial world due to their many benefits. 

Agents of Regulation of Service 

The essence of traditional financial systems is the presence of a central authority. Conventional economic systems or CeFi (Centralized finance) operate under the aegis of an umbrella authority that lists the rules and regulations of different components constituting it. In the case of DeFi, as reiterated in the previous sections, intelligent contracts are responsible for the absolute management and regulation of financial services. These smart contracts are publicly available for audit by any user. Such a facility of contract audit is not available in the case of CeFi, as the operation of financial services needs to be made public for review by a more significant number of individuals. Critics might reason against this by implicating that the presence of intelligent contracts publicly can undermine the privacy of the parties involved. In this context, DeFi ensures that the public projection of the smart contracts is pseudonymous, which means that the identities and preliminaries of the parties involved are not involved.  

Also, in the case of a financial institution acting as a platform for disseminating financial services, the culmination of financial services is delayed by the engagement of many people with different allocated responsibilities. Such a tedious and cumbersome process often drives the seekers of financial services away. Smart contracts, in the case of DeFi, ensure robustness in services with little to no human interventions in the process. 

Permissionless

CeFi and DeFi are also different regarding permissions required to process financial services. As outlined under the features of DeFi, the permissionless nature of DeFi helps economic activities to be carried out without much effort. The users can connect their existing wallets to their platform of DeFi service and engage in the requisite financial services. However, in the case of CeFi, with the requirements of KYC to avail services, the process becomes relatively longer. 

Operational Space

Another basic premise of the difference between traditional financial systems and DeFi is the operational platform. Most conventional economic systems need a physical space for their proper operation. It also requires human labor and services to keep disseminating their services in the physical area, thus necessitating a good amount spent on incentivizing human work. While DeFi is based on Distributed ledger technology (DLT) and runs effectively on blockchain networks. DeFi's entire operation employing technology and advanced software stacks eliminates the need for a formal setting in a physical space, on the one hand. It reduces the need for human labor, on the other. 

Currency

DeFi and CeFi also stand in contrast in terms of the characteristic flagship assets and their nature. For instance, DeFi, as we know, uses cryptocurrencies as all the transactions, in this case, occur on a blockchain network in a digital space. However, CeFi necessarily uses fiat currency. Thus the transactions occur with coins, paper notes, etc.   

Contract

In the case of DeFi, the interaction occurs directly between the interested parties, and the transactions subsequently take from peer to peer. Intelligent contracts support these transactions. In the context of traditional financial services, multiple points of contact or intermediaries often impede the smoothness of financial services.   

Custodianship of Funds and Assets

DeFi users have absolute custodianship of their funds and can choose when to make the transaction, with whom to make the transactions, and by how much. However, traditional financial systems do not offer such control over the funds and assets. The individual must cede their funds to the central authority in these systems. For example, banks substantiate this premise. Instead of being in control of the owners of the funds, the funds are actually under the power of the top central bank, and then the second bank follows. 

Innovativeness and Interoperability

The innovativeness also characterizes deFi that it offers in adapting to the evolving standards of expectations of the users. The streak of innovation of these systems is also substantiated by the use cases, as outlined in the next section. With innovation at its core, these systems are getting into many industries. However, due to the centralized approach, the traditional finance ecosystem only offers rapid innovation and adaptation. Due to the centralization of the authority, any proposal for creation goes through a multitude of executives at different levels in the hierarchical structure of these authorities. Such a thorough hierarchical structure ensures that the central authority makes well-deliberated decisions. While such a rigorous approach for multiple checks makes the proposal relatively risk-proof, it often leads to delay or rejection of the proposal altogether. Due to evolving trends, such a time gap might make the proposals redundant.     

# Accessibility and Reach

DeFi, being a digital finance system, is available and accessible to all individuals around the globe with the basic requirements of a stable internet connection and crypto wallet. Transactions between people sitting far apart on different continents can also successfully occur. These platforms work 24/7. At the same time, traditional financing systems are bound by time and space constraints. Due to the physical space requirements for the operation of maximum financial activities, these economical systems need to catch up to the evolved systems offered by DeFi. Additionally, due to the condition of human labor to put most financial services, activities, and transactions in motion, these systems need to be more useful in digitization. 

The DeFi systems stand in strong contrast to the conventional financial systems. DeFi has more advantages in the financial world than traditional economic systems. The utility of DeFi systems is on an exponential rise, with more and more industries attempting to find the crossroads between DeFi and its application to complement their functionality. While the DeFi systems are visibly booming with the current digitization trends, they still have a long way to go. Despite the unprecedented proliferation of technology and increased accessibility of Internet services, only 65% of the world's population has Internet access, making it impossible for them to avail the benefits these systems offer. Thus, they continue to seek financial services from intermediaries under the umbrella of traditional economic methods.  

Lack of reliable information provides concreteness to an individual's misconceptions about the working of DeFi platforms. They are falsely implicated in digital financial services as a hoax by magnifying and overdoing the risks. While it is imperative to warn the users of the dangers that might befall their digital journey in the finance world, it is essential to make them aware of the mitigation and manageableness related strategies corresponding to the risks. Additionally, many downsides of the DeFi systems have been discussed in the later sections, which contribute to tantalizing these platforms' reputation and trust quotient. Thus, even individuals accessing DeFi services refrain from venturing into these platforms. They instead turn to traditional financial service systems to receive economic benefits.  

# Use Cases of DeFi 

DeFi has taken the world by a whirlwind with its efficacy, resilience, immutability, and transparency in financial operations. It has set into motion many economic opportunities for its global user base. Due to the plethora of advantages DeFi offers, the scope of its application is already widening. It is expected to widen even further with the refinement of the system and the advancement of technology. DeFi is enabling the integration of digital finance services with multiple industries which initially didn't witness crossroads with these systems. It applies to conventional financial services like borrowing, lending, trading, marketplaces, etc. These economic systems have found application across varied sectors, from increasing the efficiency of traditional financial activities to introducing DeFi in unhackneyed industries. The use cases of this system are already growing at an unprecedented rate and are expected to follow an ascending graph line. A few prominent use cases have been delineated below. 

Borrowing and Lending

With DeFi's influence on systems of finance and transactions increasing, the loopholes in the traditional finance system are becoming all the more evident to the users. Traditional financial markets have proliferation in revenue generation by the interest they pay the lenders and the interest they charge from the borrowers. Such a system often discouraged the borrowers from approaching these markets due to rigid protocols for qualifying for a loan, including viable collateral and a hefty amount of interest charged. The digital ledger technology bolstered by DeFi opens opportunities for borrowers who need a loan and fail to procure it due to rigid requirements. With smart contracts in place, lending/borrowing loans and maintaining the terms of conditions outlined in the smart contracts smoothens the dual lending and borrowing processes. The dynamics structuring DeFi support peer-to-peer lending and borrowing. This use case is finding widespread application in the form of an increase in DeFi projects about this. 

For instance, Compound is a DeFi protocol that stands out because it has an autonomous interest basis. Compound welcomes lenders to earn interest on various cryptocurrencies. It provides the lenders with tokens, which are then siphoned off to the borrowers at algorithmically decided autonomous interest rates. The compound is highly efficient because the intelligent contract identifies the lenders and borrowers with concurring interests and lays out interest rates following the borrowed and lent assets ratio in the discussion. Another prominent example of such a project is ‘Pool Together,’ which uses akin mechanisms in lending and borrowing money.

Tokenization

One of the significant use cases of the DeFi system is in the context of tokenization. Tokenization refers to curating, issuing, regulating, and transferring digital assets on a blockchain network. Tokenization is highly pertinent because any form of investment can be converted into tokens for their storage on the blockchain. Ethereum blockchain successfully utilizes tokenization and has granted the world a secure and easy means of trading in the form of Ethereum-based tokens. Out of the many advantages conferred by the usage of tickets, the main benefits emerge as fractional aspects of these tokens and available options for their customization. Thus tokenization is one of the central features of the booming DeFi ecosystem due to its digital alternatives and efficacy in complementing trade.   

Efficacy of Stablecoins

One of the significant downsides of cryptocurrency is the profound volatility that is widespread in the crypto ecosystem. Such fluctuations of prices that experience frequent ascent and descent in their value result in the reduction of the reliability of cryptocurrency. Stablecoins pose as a savior in this context. Stablecoin can be understood as a type of unique cryptocurrency because it is tied with an or bulk of them. These assets can be anything ranging from fiat to gold or even any other kind of cryptocurrency. Their consolidation with acquisitions offers stability in contrast to the volatility in the crypto market. They also provide the blockchain greater credibility as a reliable payment method. Stablecoins are now used for borrowing, lending, etc., in the DeFi ecosystem. 

Process of Asset Management 

DeFi allows users to exercise greater control over their assets. With no central authority for regulation, Decentralization helps the users gain absolute control of their assets. It will enable the users to manage their assets according to their volition. It can involve buying, selling, lending, or transferring digital assets. By removing an intermediary, only the users can access and manage their assets in any way they deem perfect. For example, in many DeFi projects, including Gnosis Safe, Argent facilitates the users' complete control of their information and ensures safe data storage with the user only. 

Application in the Insurance Industry

Another major use case of DeFi is in the insurance industry. DeFi is revolutionizing the insurance industry by eliminating the significant limitations posed by the traditional handling of insurance systems. Such rules include unfathomable amounts of paperwork, the flawed claim handling process, time-consuming, etc. The insurance industry also experiences setbacks due to the fraudulent practices of false claims. Blockchain's decentralized technology can verify the presented documents, ownership, policy registration-related details, and claim authenticity. With smart contracts in place, the process of financial exchange as a result of insurance becomes easier for both the seeker and the insurance company.  

Compliance Supported by the KYT mechanism 

Know-your-customers (KYC) protocols majorly guided compliance in countering-the-financing-of-terrorism and anti-money laundering (AML). However, KYC protocols often interfered with DeFi's premise of privacy. It also did involve the external authority at one step or another contradicting the decentralization aspect of DeFi. Therefore, Defi has introduced a relatively evolved and better Know-Your-Transaction (KYT) mechanism. This mechanism lays the ground for the focus to shift from the identity of the user to digital addresses and the transaction behavior of the users. Such a mechanism assesses the live behavior of transactions and maintains the users' privacy. This mechanism helps assess risk or crisis proliferating in the system, early detection of which can combat eventual large-scale frauds and siphoning of hefty amounts.  

Decentralized Exchanges

Decentralized exchanges (abbreviated as DEXs) refer to the transactions of cryptocurrency that occur P2P (peer-to-peer) without the involvement of any central authority, thus supporting the basic premise of DeFi. The assets in such exchanges are non-custodial to any external exchange platform. It allows users to interact, transact and maintain control of their assets. The significant advantage conferred is in terms of mitigation of risks. Since the assets are not under the direct supervision of any external authority or platform, it reduces the chances of hacking, fraud, and price manipulation. Currently, many DEXs are used in the user-friendly and easily accessible DeFi space. The most popular ones are Liquality, AirSwap, Mesa, etc.  

Bolstered Digital Identity

Digital identity systems based on blockchain are coming to focus as viable and efficient identity systems. Mainly when amalgamated with the DeFi protocols, these identity systems can experience a boost in their efficiency in terms of immediate processing when required to avail of financial services. These systems are also changing the indicators of creditworthiness. According to the traditional finance systems, the hands to authenticate the credibility of financial service seekers used to be their possession of assets (owned houses) and income level. However, the digital identity reinforced by DeFi and blockchain protocols authenticates the seekers by their transactional behavior, financial activities, online reputation, or any other attributes that deem practical and necessary. This helps financial service seekers overcome the hindrances from collateralization and receive requisite services based on indicators they are mindful of. 

Risk Mitigation and Management 

The flagship features of DeFi highlight transparency and Decentralization, which have, in turn, become highly crucial for mitigating risks arising on the respective platform. This is done by thorough data analysis that the users can engage in with the data repository provided to them. Following their data analysis and being well informed about the risks associated with and predicted, the users can make decisions of their volition. Such comprehensive frameworks help users know the risks and ways to mitigate them. It also keeps the users updated about the financial opportunities arising in the market. The proliferation of DeFi has brought tools like DeFi Pulse and CoDeFi Data to light, which are involved in the processes mentioned above.

DeFi and Prediction Markets 

Despite the critical views regarding the ethics of predicting and betting, these attract many people. The prediction markets allow the users to place bets or vote in favor or against the outcome of an event that hasn't occurred. Events under the umbrella of predictive use cases include predictions regarding sports events and election outcomes, to name a few. The prediction markets' functionality resonates with the traditional predictive needs regarding function only.

The amalgamation of blockchain with these markets eliminates the need for an intermediary. There are many contenders for the top spots in the list of DeFi prediction markets—for instance, Augur, Gnosis, Polymarket, Predictit, FTX, etc. Augur has emerged as the strongest contender on the list. This platform specializes in prediction and allows users to bet on political or economic events. DeFi-backed prediction markets were spotlighted in 2020 during the U.S. presidential elections due to the number of users involved in predicting results and the magnitude of bets in terms of values. Augur was reported to record the amount of about $8 million during these elections.

Gaming and DeFi: The Wonder of no-loss games

An essential feature of DeFi is the composability that the system offers. This composability has motivated the product developers to design protocols of DeFi into the platform itself. The latest video games have options for in-app purchasing, which provokes the users to use the live currency to purchase characters, elements, or other aspects of the game. The developers in the field of gaming are integrating their area with the DeFi systems by incentivizing the playing users.

Curating reward models often do this to target the relish points of the users utilizing DeFi coins. For example, PoolTogether is a game that is based on the premise of no loss. The users are indicated to submit the DAI stablecoin, purchasing digital tickets. These stablecoins are pooled together and stashed away till the end of the month. At the end of every month, this stash again resurfaces to award one player who earned the interest, and the deposited amounts are returned to all the players.

Exchange of synthetic assets 

Synthetic assets can be understood as tokenized derivative that works much like another asset in value and functionality without having any actual physical relationship with the asset. These crypto-assets indicate assets of other kinds like fiat, gold, etc. These synthetic assets can be easily used as collateral by tokens in intelligent contracts with predetermined rules, regulations, and agreements. The understanding of synthetic assets can be supplemented by the example of a platform that allows users to curate and trade tailor-made synthetic asset versions. The Synthetix platform enables the exchange of synthetic versions of various assets like gold, fiat, commodities, etc. 

# Efficient trading practices

The DeFi ecosystem includes many transactional activities, from exchanging synthetic assets to exchanging tokens and margin trading. The advantage conferred by DeFi to the trading industry is the reduced reliance on brokers for trading to be initiated. Crypto traders also relish the benefits of DEXs due to reduced exchange charges, quick transactions, and absolute custody of the assets to the owner. 

Blockchain-Based Online Marketplaces

DeFi has enabled digital spaces for the proliferation of marketplaces that link interested buyers and sellers. These markets are widening in their scope and magnitude as well. These digital markets drive out the need for an intermediary and brokers. This allows the sellers of many commodities to access the global user base of DeFi platforms without listing on retail brokerage platforms like Amazon. A significant part of online markets is supported by DeFi protocols enabling the exchange of products and services via peer-to-peer transactions. As mentioned above, the scope of these marketplaces is widening due to the advances in the technology corresponding to DeFi. It can also be attributed to the increasing applications and advantages of DeFi. These cater from established retail products like apparel to new business models like freelance gig platforms. For example, freelance gig hubs like Freelancer and Upwork are witnessing competition from platforms like Anytask built on the blockchain. The advantage that this blockchain platform of Anytask brings is that, unlike Freelancer and Upwork, it does not charge the service sellers any fee for accessing the gigs on their forum. 

As outlined above, DeFi finds applications in various cases like borrowing and lending, asset management, insurance cases, stablecoins, compliance supported by KYT mechanisms, trading, no-loss gaming systems, etc. Despite our attempts to include the majority of use cases of DeFi, many use cases still need to be found mentioned in the section above. DeFi, a relatively new system, constantly evolves in its dynamics and application to different sectors. With such an evolving nature, the scopes of applications keep changing with new additions with its proliferation. Nonetheless, the objective of encompassing use cases in the narrative was to highlight the applications of DeFi and to give the readers an idea of how widespread DeFi is becoming, including industry after industry under its umbrella.

# Significant Considerations of DeFi

Even though the DeFi systems offer a vast array of advantageous facets due to the culmination of finance and blockchain networks on DLT, it is still fraught with many risks and limitations, which the users should be aware of before venturing into the DeFi ecosystem. With the increasing applications industries, DeFi has also become home to cyber perpetrators on these platforms. Reports published by CipherTrace (a firm involved in crypto analytics) brought to light threatening figures of crypto crimes. According to this report, DeFi thefts in 2020 alone were about $100 million. In the latter half of 2020, about half of the cryptocurrency thefts were due to DeFi protocols. These staggering figures indicate the risks associated with DeFi. In addition to these risks, this system has many downsides, which will be explained in the sections below. 

Before delineating the cons of this system, it is pertinent to note a few things. To begin with, the more significant part of the downsides of DeFi is because of the technology upon which they are built. The challenges of the base technology, in turn, propels the minor downsides of DeFi to become profound in their impact. If the blockchain network or technology that DeFi apps are built on suffers from issues of instability. If the base technology they are built upon is stable, the subsequently developed protocols will also be safe. Additionally, some of the risks associated with DeFi can be mitigated and managed if the users know them.  

Risks Arising Due to Smart Contracts 

The first and foremost disadvantage branches from the essential components of this system: smart contracts. Although intelligent contracts have eased the process of exchanging financial services, ensuring high reliability, even then, it is fraught with a few technical limitations. Smart contracts, at their outset, are at the disposal of humans, as the lines of the code are written by an individual only before its public upload on the blockchain. This introduces the first instance of the possibility of human error. No matter how strict a smart contract is, it can still have many loopholes. The availability of these smart contracts on the public blockchain offers the content multiple sets of eyes to skim through the contract for the slightest loopholes. While just a few individuals form an intelligent agreement, it is available for audit to the global database accessing it. Such math opens up opportunities for exploitation of the loopholes present in the smart contract. Even a single loophole can be misused to drain millions from the smart contract.

Since smart contracts are available on the public blockchain, prospective investors can review them themselves or often outsource the review to security firms for auditing. This brings to light the second possibility of losses due to human error. Humans can miss out on loopholes in the contract, which then leads to exploitation in the future and financial losses eventually.  

For example, Decentralization Autonomous Organization (DAO) was built on an open-source code allowing absolute autonomy and supplementing Decentralization. Launched in April 2016, it was a venture capital fund directed by investors. It saw exponential growth, which soon made this platform one of the most significant crowdfunding ventures, collecting around $120 million. By June 2016, however, an array of hackers emerged who identified a few flaws and vulnerabilities in the smart contract. These vulnerabilities helped the hackers in siphoning off about $50 million. The siphoned amount made it impossible for investors to access their funds for weeks. This became the biggest hack in the history of crowdfunding.

Efforts are being made to prevent the exploitation of smart contracts. For instance, Nexus Mutual is a team focused on structuring aspects of decentralized insurance that will help ensure the interests and security of the users in case they are faced with scams and frauds by cyber perpetrators on these systems. 

Hacking and Frauds

The proliferation of fraud occurs in a way that often goes unnoticed at the outset and comes to the spotlight in retrospect. There have been reports of funds laundering on DeFi platforms like Kyber Network, Uniswap, etc. As explained in the previous sections, another source for fraud to occur is hacking by identifying the loopholes in the smart contract that are available on the blockchain network. Such activities put shade on the security of the funds on the DeFi platforms, raising concerns in the context of the trust quotient. While this may lead to users losing their money, on the one hand, it discourages non-existing users from switching from traditional financial systems to DeFi. 

Problems with the Scalability of the Host Platforms 

The DeFi protocols are well-suited for ensuring the inclusion of more people to cater to their financial services irrespective of geographic impediments. However, blockchain-based projects need to improve the scalability of these host platforms. DeFi suffers as the transactions take longer to process after confirmation. This can be attributed to the fact that these DeFi systems can process a definite set number of transactions at a single moment. For instance, the DeFi-based platform - Ethereum, even at its total capacity, can only process about 13 transactions per second. At the same time, traditional centralized financial systems can allow many transactions in the same period without any error. Therefore, despite the ease offered by DeFi in initiating a transaction, it needs substantial waiting periods for the confirmation of the transaction. Additionally, transaction costs increase at times of such congestion. 

The basic premise of DeFi systems is bringing Decentralization to the table and replacing the conventional financial plans with the central authority as an impediment. This Decentralization provides transparency, flexibility, and accessibility, increasing the trustworthiness of DeFi projects and reducing the possibilities of fraud and scandals. However, despite the claims, not all services of DeFi systems are decentralized. For example, a critical use case of these systems in the form of stablecoins introduces the role of central authority either directly in the case of fiat currency-based stablecoins or indirectly in the case of commodity-backed stablecoins. 

Apart from the abovementioned issues, many other problems inflect the DeFi systems. To begin with, the anonymity DeFi systems provide can often accelerate the chances of scams and fraud. With the public space open to all users around the globe, the risk factor becomes prominent. Since the DeFi platform does not require KYC from its users, it often includes the bad actors on the forum who indulge in malpractices. Although the verification systems used by DeFi are efficient, robust, and less time-consuming, they can often be misleading. Since authentication is facilitated by tracking online behavior, transactional activities, and other such practical attributes, these attributes can be manipulated, thus tricking the authentication system. 

Secondly, insurance is given requisite attention in the case of centralized finance due to its necessity in ensuring the customers' trust and protecting them from events of hacking or fraud which might lead the customers to incur substantial financial losses. However, the aspect of insurance in the case of DeFi is rarely given due importance, if ever. Also, such insurances demand a place, especially when the users have to take responsibility solely.

Even if DeFi has no associated risks or issues, the responsibility for everything will be on the user's shoulders. With the complexity of the operation, room for mistakes is always there, especially for users with less experience with operating technology or those who have just joined DeFi platforms. Their likelihood of losing their funds due to a tiny mistake causes them significant financial problems. Thus, more work is required in the DeFi space to make the platform user-friendly and create tools that minimize the risks of potential human errors.  

Despite the risks and limitations of DeFi, it is still booming as it continues to offer advantages to various industries in the form of its successful and efficient applications. Especially after 2020, these systems must focus due to the increasing number of locked assets. There has also been an increase in the use cases of DeFi, which has yielded more recognition of the system. Even though the technology used by DeFi requires refinement in terms of the necessary tools to reduce the associated risks, nonetheless, it is expected to reign in the financial world due to its interoperability and innovativeness, amongst other factors.

# Chapter 6: Non-Fungible Tokens

# What are NFTs?

Non-Fungible Tokens or NFTs, as the name suggests, are tokens, much like other crypto tokens such as Bitcoin or ether; however, the critical difference is that they are non-fungible, meaning they cannot be copied or replicated. Each of the tokens is unique by itself. E.g., when you buy a cryptocurrency, such as a Bitcoin, and then you exchange it for another Bitcoin, in the end, you have the same thing; there is no way to differentiate between the Bitcoin that you were holding earlier and the Bitcoin that you are holding now, this means that they are fungible.

Non-Fungible tokens are unique because if you exchange one NFT of similar value with another NFT, then what you have in your hand is something different. The fungibility of currencies and cryptocurrencies usually helps us use them as a medium of exchange since everyone has an agreed value common to all the units of the said currency. NFTs, on the other hand, can be compared to collectibles such as unique sports cards or art pieces.

NFTs use blockchain technology to register the transaction, typically, it is the Ethereum blockchain, but other blockchains are also used, which keeps track of the ownership of the NFT. NFTs are created on these blockchains using the ERC-721 and ERC-1155 frameworks. More often than not, NFTs are artwork such as images, GIFs, videos, and music. What happens is that the person purchasing that original piece of art has proof of their ownership, the transaction, stored on the blockchain. Since we know that blockchain has immutable and permanent data, the transaction cannot be altered and is thus a set proof of the fact that the person owns that particular asset.

The value of most things is directly proportional to the scarcity of the said thing. When we say that gold and diamonds are expensive, they are only available in a limited amount, and we cannot create any more natural gold or diamonds. Similarly, art pieces such as those by Picasso, Monet, Banksy, or Basquiat carry astronomical values during auctions and other sales because they are only a limited number of such pieces. Further, each piece is unique from the other. Before the advent of blockchain technology and NFTs, there was no such scarcity of digital art forms, one could search for a song or a photograph and they would get thousands of copies of the same. With NFTs, however, there is a newfound scarcity in the ownership of digital assets, which increases the value of these digital assets. 

NFTs work as financial assets; they can be compared to physical art, except they are digital art forms. Just like physical art has no intrinsic value, and most people purchase it either for the bragging rights of having the ownership of the said piece of art or for using it as an investment which will give them returns when they sell it off at a higher value in the future, NFTs are similarly either owned to be flipped for profit in the future or for the fact that one holds the original version of a digital asset.

What one needs to understand, however, is the fact that just because they have ownership of an NFT doesn’t mean that the same digital art can’t be reproduced by anyone else. Think of it as a painting, such as The Starry Night by Vincent Van Gogh; At the same time, various painters made multiple copies of The Starry Night, and it is even available as posters or drawing aids; the ownership of the original Starry Night lies in the hands of a single person, or this case, the Rijks museum. Similarly, the same gif or image will be available online on various web pages or search engines. Still, the original art lies in the hands of the owner of the non-fungible token.

When a person purchases art using an NFT, the artist or the original owner encrypts digital information and metadata onto the blockchain. This data is used to prove the ownership of the NFT. Further, since blockchains have timestamps, one could even trace back the right of the digital asset to see when and how many times it has changed hands over time. 

NFTs can be traded like any other digital assets; there are markets for NFTs where one can go and purchase the token they like. Any fixed market force does not set the price for NFTs; much like art, their price is determined by what the buyer is willing to pay for the asset. NFTs can even be programmed so that the owner is paid a certain amount every time the NFT changes hands, so in case the price of the NFT skyrockets, the original owner is still compensated. NFTs use distributed ledger technology such as blockchain, which means there is no need for an intermediary or an exchange for the transfer of NFTs, bringing down the transaction costs.

NFTs have little to no regulations in the global markets, and their future is very much connected with cryptocurrencies and blockchain technology. For example, if cryptocurrency trade is banned in a particular country, then the sale and purchase of NFTs will also be restricted there.  

While NFTs have become increasingly popular, their criticisms have also increased. One of the significant criticism associated with NFTs is their carbon footprint. The world is currently dealing with a climatic disaster partly fuelled by using non-renewable and polluting energy sources. We know that blockchain is a very energy-intensive industry. It takes electricity to run the servers responsible for mining on the blockchains. NFTs, to be created also need similar mining requirements, causing pollution and climate change. Some artists have pulled out of making their NFTs after getting to know about the environmental impact of NFTs.

# Properties of Non-fungible Tokens

Non-fungible tokens have certain features which help it function the way it does. Some of these features are:

Unique: Every NFT is unique, i.e., an NFT cannot be copied. Unlike other cryptocurrencies, one NFT does not equate to another NFT, even if they are of the same value. If one trades NFTs, they have something different in their hand and not just a separate copy of the same thing. 

Indivisible: NFTs are also indivisible, which means that NFTs cannot be broken down into smaller parts of lesser value and then sold, collected again, and reassembled. An NFT is always traded as a whole. 

Indestructible: The data for an NFT is stored on blockchains. Most of the NFTs are stored on the Ethereum blockchain. Since we know that the data on blockchains are tough to destroy or alter, especially on public blockchains, the NFTs cannot be destroyed. 

Absolute ownership: When a person owns an NFT, they carry total ownership over the digital asset; it is not like purchasing the license to use a particular image, song, or gif. This means that the owner has unrestricted access to these NFTs.

Verifiable: Since the data regarding the ownership of an NFT is stored on blockchains and such data is always timestamped and, at the same time, assured of authenticity, it is easy to verify and trace the ownership of an NFT. 

Limited: NFTs are limited in number; they are scarce. Most NFTs are only produced singularly; however, an artist may have a limited number of similar products, such as 10 or 50, much like little edition clothes or cars by real-life manufacturers.

Transferable: NFTs are digital assets or certificates that pronounce the ownership of digital assets; hence like any other digital asset, NFTs can be transferred from one person to another for a price.

Permanent: The data stored on blockchains are endless; once entered, it is always there. Unlike real-life paintings or art forms that might get spoilt over time, the NFTs will be permanent.

# History of Non-fungible Tokens

The idea behind non-fungible tokens started with something called “colored coins” in 2012, which were being offered on the Bitcoin blockchain. These colored coins denote the real-life ownership of cars, metals, real estate, etc.

Non-fungible Tokens were first minted in the year 2014 by Kevin Mckoy. He coined the first non-fungible token called “quantum” before using NFTs extensively in crypto art. Quantum is a pixelated image of an octagonal object that changes shapes and colors.

The NFTs started catching public attention after the introduction of CryptoKitties on the Ethereum blockchain and that of crypto punks. Crypto kitties are non-fungible tokens depicting cats, each with unique age, size, and color features. It means that none of the cats are the same; they are thus traded as collectibles among people, much like sports cards or Pokémon cards in real life. Cryptopunks, conversely, are avatars or profile pictures that have been converted into NFTs, which people can use for various social media platforms. Again, each cyberpunk avatar differs from the others and has its value. The NFTs were growing in the crypto community, but they entered a mainstream boom in 2021.

# The NFT Revolution in 2021

In the year 2021, the interest in NFTs ballooned to unprecedented heights. While this was in line with the bull market that saw the prices of cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin, Ethereum, and even dogecoin during the first quarter of the year, it was observed that despite the fall in the prices of these cryptocurrencies from their peaks in the second quarter of the year there was continued growth in the sale and purchase of non-fungible tokens during the same period. 

The NFT market has grown by leaps and bounds in 2021. In the first half of 2021, compared to the 13.7 million dollars in sales in the first half of 2020, the NFT sales had surged to an almost unbelievable 2.5 billion dollars. Within this, we saw an increase in Quarter 1 to Quarter 2 of about 114.6%. In this same period, there was a substantial increase in the number of wallets that store NFTs, at about 151.89%. NFT trading platforms such as Open Sea have also increased, with Open Sea currently worth around 1.5 billion dollars. Sales of NFTs have moved on from being only on trading platforms to being made by traditional auction houses such as Sotheby’s and Christie’s. 

It is not just the quantum of NFT sales that has increased in 2021. The value of the NFTs also increased substantially, with a majority of the most expensive NFT sales ever made being in 2021. In February, we saw digital art by the musician Grimes being sold for an amount nearing 6 million dollars, while the GIF for Nyan cat fetched a price of almost half a million dollars or 300 ethers. In March, the artwork by Beeple, pseudonym of the digital artist Michael Winkelmann, called “Every Day: The first 5000 Days” was listed by the auction house Christie’s and sold for a record price of 69.3 million dollars. The same month, Jack Dorsey, Twitter's founder, and CEO, sold his first-ever tweet after converting it into an NFT. This tweet was sold for a price of 2.9 million dollars. Similarly, the cost of various avatars by Cyberpunks has continuously been sold for prices ranging from a few hundred thousand dollars to 7.6 million dollars or 4200 Ether for CryptoPunks #7804.

Some of the most popular NFTs that one should know about are:

CryptoKitties: Crypto kitties were one of the first NFTs readily traded by the public. CryptoKitties were developed by the Canadian development studio called Dapper Labs in 2017. Since then, the popularity and the price of the various cats in the game have increased manifolds, with some rarer cats, such as “dragon,” costing more than a million US dollars. Crypto Kitties is a game based on the Ethereum blockchain. Users can purchase, breed, and sell cats in this game.

The cats in the game are all unique. Each cat has its color, size, name, and other characteristics which cannot be copied. The users own the cats and thus cannot be taken away by anyone, including the game’s developers. The cats can also be used as collectibles, wherein people collect a specific type of cat and then display these collections. Depending on their attributes and rarity, a few different types of cats are in the game, such as Exclusive Cats, Fancy Cats, and Special Edition Cats.

CryptoPunks: Cryptopunks are the other path setters in Non-fungible Tokens, along with CryptoKitties. Much like CryptoKitties, crypto punks were also introduced in 2017. Cryptopunks were created by the American development studio called Larva Labs. The cyberpunk movement inspired the creation of crypto punks. The crypto punks, in turn, inspired the ER-721 standard of Ethereum that is now used in most digital art NFTs.

Crypto punks are avatars that people can use to display pictures on various platforms. In total, 10,000 crypto punks were created by Larva Labs. Of these 10,000 crypto punks, 6039 are male, 3840 are female, 88 are zombies, 24 are apes, and 9 are aliens. Each of these crypto punks is unique and thus carries its value. The price of the crypto punk depends on the rarity; with the nine alien crypto punks being the rarest, they have been purchased at the highest prices till now.

Crypto punks regularly feature on the list of most expensive NFTs sold, with CryptoPunks #7804 selling for 7.6 million dollars, CryptoPunks #6965 selling for 1.6 million dollars, and CryptoPunks #4156 selling for 1.3 million dollars. Even mainstream companies have started purchasing punks, with Visa shelling out almost 176,000 dollars on a crypto punk in July 2021.

Rare Pepes: Rare pipes were the first NFTs that were offered on the platform known as counterparty. Pepes are images of green-colored frogs that are edited in various ways. The Pepe images of different kinds have been converted to NFTs and then sold to the highest bidder. The ‘rarest’ of pipes, a picture of Homer Pepe, i.e., the famous TV character of Homer Simpson, edited to look like a Pepe, was brought for a price of 320,000 dollars in March 2021

Everyday: The first 5000 days and other Beeple arts: The first 5000 days are the most expensive NFT to have been sold till now. It was sold for 69.3 million dollars or 42329 Ether during an auction by the auction house Christie’s. It is a collage of five thousand images created by Beeple. It was purchased by Vignesh Sundaresan, who is now displaying it in a digital museum.

It is not only the most expensive digital art piece till now but also one of the most expensive pieces sold by a living artist. Beeple is the pseudonym of the American digital artist Mike Winkelmann, who has converted many of his digital pieces into NFTs and sold them. His artworks are some of the most in-demand NFTs in the crypto world, with most selling in minutes and charging exorbitant prices. Another one of his pieces, called Crossroads, a 10-second video, sold for approximately 6.6 million dollars in February 2021.

Decentraland: Decentraland is a 3-D virtual reality world. In this world, the products, lands, and other amenities are sold as NFTs, connecting the virtual and real worlds. It was initially started by Argentinian developers in 2015. It slowly moved from a pixelated sphere to a 3-d world. Recently, the prices of virtual real estate have increased manifolds, with some of the prime real estate in decentral costing as much as 100,000 dollars. Real-world companies such as Sotheby’s and Republic Realm are purchasing digital real estate in this world to use as digital offices and shopping places. 

Alchemy Toys: Alchemy Toys is a game based on the Binance Smart chain, developed by a German software company called Backupdev UG. It is being promoted as an amalgamation of non-fungible tokens, massively multiplayer online role-playing games, and decentralized finance. In this game, players can own unique and rare alchemy toys, which can then be combined to form newer toys or sold to the other players for the actual value. It is also possible to sacrifice or burn these toys. If a person offers all the different toys, they are rewarded with Binance coins.

Pancake Swap: Pancake swap is also based on the Binance bright chain. On the platform, the users are provided with bunnies that are NFTs. The bunnies have no purpose other than being decorative, except that the users can also trade them for Cake coins, the cryptocurrency of the said platform.

Gods Unchained: Gods Unchained is much like real-life card trading games. In the game, the players are given actual ownership of their cards which are tradeable NFTs. These cards, when sold, provide the players with cryptocurrencies and real value. The value of the cards depends on the rarity of these cards, with some costing upwards of ten thousand dollars. The game was created by Immutable, a development studio based in Australia specializing in blockchain games.

# Differences Between NFTs and Typical Cryptocurrencies

Before we look at how non-fungible tokens differ from the otherwise mainstream or typical cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin, Ether, or Cardano, we must first understand their similarities and what connects them. NFTs and cryptocurrencies are, in essence, digital assets that can be sold or purchased on online platforms with little to no inherent or intrinsic value. Both NFTs and cryptocurrencies are based on blockchain or distributed ledger technologies. The transaction history in both cases is stored on blockchains and is permanent and immutable. NFTs, much like cryptocurrencies, are also in a grey area when it comes to regulation in most countries.

Nature: The first point of difference that we see between NFTs and cryptocurrencies is their essential nature. When we talk about cryptocurrencies, we consider them, as the name suggests, as currencies, i.e., they are to be used as tools or money-like instruments that will be used for purchasing other things or assets. However, in some places and by some people, they are treated as investments that might grow in value and then be sold off. On the other hand, when we talk about NFTs, as earlier discussed, they are not currencies despite being tokens. NFTs are like assets in nature. They are not to be used for purchasing other stuff but rather as collectibles. While cryptocurrencies can be compared to money in real life, NFTs can be compared to works of Art and Luxury.  

Fungibility: The most crucial characteristic of NFTs that differentiates them from typical cryptocurrencies is the one that is in their name, i.e., fungibility. Fungibility is the ability of similar goods or assets to be interchangeable or replaced by an identical item. Fungibility is an essential feature of currency. It ensures that people can freely use cash anywhere, and there is no difference between one unit of money with another. Cryptocurrencies like fiat currencies are fungible. If you trade one ether for another, there is no difference between the two; you essentially have the same thing in hand. On the other hand, NFTs are not fungible; each one is unique and thus cannot be copied. If you exchange one NFT for another, you have something different now than you had earlier.

Value determinant: The way NFTs are determined is also different from how the value of cryptocurrencies is determined. Since cryptocurrencies have a very high volume of sales and exchange hands very frequently, thus the price of the cryptocurrencies is determined by the prevailing market forces, i.e., demand and supply. NFTs are unique, and each carries a different value; further, the price of the NFT is determined by what the buyer is willing to pay for the said product. In the case of NFTs, the market's supply side is constant. 

Cost: The cost or price of NFTs and cryptocurrencies also differ to a great extent. When we talk about cryptocurrencies, the most popular and expensive cryptocurrency is Bitcoin. Bitcoin, during the bull market of 2021, reached a peak price of just above 64,000 dollars. This, however, completes pales in comparison to the kind of prices that people have paid for some NFTs. People regularly pay upwards of a million dollars for NFTs, with the most expensive NFT netting around 69 million dollars during an auction in March 2021.

Divisibility: Another difference between NFTs and cryptocurrencies is their ability to be divided into smaller pieces. Cryptocurrencies can be broken down into pieces, which can further be broken down into parts. This is essential for expensive cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin so people can purchase them in smaller quantities. NFTs always exist as a whole, and they are like art pieces. They can't be broken down, traded, collected, and reassembled.  

Blockchain used: The blockchains that are used for the two also differ. While for cryptocurrencies, almost every cryptocurrency runs on its blockchain, which stores the transactions of the said currency. For example, the trades of Ether are recorded on Ethereum. Whereas NFTs are more often than not running on the ether blockchain, some of them are also on the Binance bright chain. These are the two most commonly used blockchains for NFTs.

Purchase platforms: The platforms at which the two are traded are also different. At the same time, Cryptocurrencies are traded on crypto exchanges, such as Binance and Coinbase. These crypto exchanges can be both centralized or decentralized in nature. NFTs are bought and sold on NFT platforms like Open Sea and Nifty Gateway. At the same time, NFTs are also being sold by auction houses such as Sotheby’s and Christie’s.

Currencies Accepted: The money that can be paid for the purchase of the two is also different. When we talk about cryptocurrencies, one can pay for them in fiat currencies or other cryptocurrencies, depending on their crypto exchange. Thus we see that cryptocurrencies are readily convertible to real-world money. On the other hand, to purchase NFTs, one has to pay only in cryptocurrencies. If the NFT is based on the Ethereum blockchain, it is paid for in Ethers (ETH), and if it is on the Binance Smart Chain, then it is paid for in Binance coins (BNB)

Timeline: The timeline of the emergence of the two is also very different. Cryptocurrencies were first created in 2008, the first being Bitcoin. Cryptocurrencies came into the mainstream in the middle to late 2010s, with the first bull market in 2017 and the second in early 2021. On the other hand, the first NFT was introduced in 2016, it became slightly famous in 2017 due to crypto kitties and punks, but it only entered the mainstream in 2021 when the NFT market ultimately ballooned. 

# Workings of NFTs

Creating an NFT is relatively easy; various platforms allow people to make an NFT.
