To make a non-fungible token, one needs two things, the digital art or asset that will be converted into a non-fungible permit and the payment for doing the minting. Recently, much stuff has been converted into non-fungible tokens, including digital art, songs, videos, and even tweets; anything with a digital form or signature can be converted into an NFT. Once created, the NFTs can be viewed by the owner on wallets, just like cryptocurrencies. These NFTs similarly exist on addresses on the blockchain, which can be accessed by the person holding the key. 

During the minting process of an NFT, the owner or creator of the NFT executes code that conforms to different NFT standards. NFTs use standards and protocols such as the following:

ERC - 721: ERC - 721 was the original standard developed to create NFTs in 2017. It was written in Solidity on the Ethereum blockchain. This standard allows minting new NFTs easier, faster, and non-repetitive. ERC-721 is the most commonly used standard; anyone can create their own NFT.

ERC - 1155: ERC - 1155 is another standard on the Ethereum blockchain. It was developed after ERC-721 and is considered an upgrade over the previous one. The bar has smart contracts executed in a way that makes new non-fungible tokens. One of the significant advantages that ERC - 1155 has over ERC - 721 is that one can create multiple NFTs on a single, smart contract. On an ERC-721, one had to run the intelligent contract for a new NFT every time. The ERC-1155 is more efficient and takes less storage space, but it is also more diverse since it can make fungible tokens and non-fungible receipts. 

Hedera Token Service - This is a non-Ethereum-based standard that Hedera offers. This is much cheaper than the Ethereum standards and also faster. Infinite by SUKU is one company that has shifted to Hedera from Ethereum.

Currently, we see that NFTs are proprietary and have single owners. There are plans to introduce fractional NFT standards shortly, allowing multiple users to own a fraction of a single NFT. This would align with the decentralizing and multiple-node nature of blockchains in general. 

# NFT Use Cases

NFTs can be used in various sectors. Some of these use cases are:

Gaming: Non-fungible tokens, primarily online gaming, are extensively used in the gaming sector. Some of the first NFTs, such as CryptoKitties, were just games set up on blockchains. NFTs can be used in gaming in two broad ways, either the collections and storing of the NFTs is in itself a game, and people are trying to get NFTs, convert them into something else within the game, and then try to win a reward, as is the case in crypto kitties or alchemy toys. Conversely, it is also possible that the NFTs are being used as appendages to other games. These can be cosmetic (skins, avatars, helmets, etc.) and utile (weapons, pets, etc.). 

In Massively Multiplayer Online Role-Playing games, people can use NFTs to own certain in-game items, giving them the exclusive right to use these. The gaming industry is one of the fastest-growing industries in the world. With the increase in mobile-based games and internet penetration in previously untapped regions, it is set to expand even further in the next few years. The gaming industry will be worth over 300 billion dollars by 2023. This provides NFTs with a large market base and potential growth in this sector.

Digital Assets: The digital asset marketplace is also one of these tokens' most critical use cases. It is possible to purchase assets that only exist in the digital world. Decentraland is among the most common names when buying digital assets as NFTs. Decentraland is a virtual world with a certain amount of finite and scarce land. A prospective buyer can purchase this land from others or even the developers. The price of the land is dependent, much like in the real world, on the location of this ‘plot.’ Some of the game's prime location digital real estate is being sold for thousands of dollars. Companies are planning to set up their digital stores in these spaces. ‘Cryptovoxel’ is another project where people can purchase and sell digital assets.

Art: Currently, Art is the sector where NFTs are used most. Digital art for years was plagued with the problem of being easily copied, and thus with no scarcity for the said product, the price of digital art could have been higher. This completely changed after people started converting their digital art to NFTs. When Art was combined with NFT, it became a lot easier for the owners of the said pieces to authenticate their ownership. This created a limited supply of digital art, which caused it to shoot in value. Over the last couple of years, we have seen some digital paintings being sold for astronomical prices, such as every day: The First 500 Days, which was sold at 69 million dollars. 

However, it is not just famous artists like Beeple profiting from NFTs. Even smaller artists can convert their art into NFTs and thus monetize their work. Further, using smart contracts, it is also possible for the artist to attach a clause to the NFT that gives them a cut of the price every time an NFT is sold to protect their economic interest if their work gets popular and increases in price in the future. NFTs would also remove the need for mediators and other intermediaries between the artists and the art collectors. NFTs could finally bring the digital art world on par with the physical art world.

Identification: Non-fungible tokens are also being used for providing proof of identity. NFTs can be programmed to contain unique information within them, and at the same time, we know that NFTs can only have a single owner at a time, and the data within an NFT cannot be mutated; thus, NFTs can be configured in such a way to issue identities, licenses, qualifications, etc. For example, suppose a person is to be given an identity card for club membership. In that case, the digital version of the card can be configured with an NFT to ensure that it is the original card shown upon entry. In case of identity theft, even if the culprit can copy the identification cards, they will not be able to copy the NFT that is attached to these cards. 

Collectibles: Another of the most common and popular uses of NFTs is currently in the collectibles sector. We know that NFTs are non-fungible and unique tokens, meaning that every NFT differs. Collectibles had been absent in digital form as they could easily be copied and duplicated, but this problem has been solved with NFTs. It is now possible to create an item in a finite amount. Thus even if a product is copied, its authenticity cannot be proven. This creates a form of exclusivity in the ownership of the said items. Some of the collectibles that are being produced with NFTs are:

Trading Cards: For years, trading cards have been one of the most sorts after collectibles in the real world. Trading cards can be of many types, such as for sports like baseball or basketball trading cards or in the form of games such as Pokémon or yu-gi-ho. The rarer cards have sometimes been sold for millions of dollars. Now we are seeing the start of digital trading card games like Gods Unchained.

Avatars: Crypto punks were one of the first NFTs and have become some of the most popular ones. The purchase of the limited edition alien crypto punks has regularly exceeded the value of a million dollars. These punks have no intrinsic value but are just being as collectible because of their rarity. One can use them as avatars or display pictures on various platforms. 

Original Copies: Just like the original documents of various products in the real world, such as comic books, novels, and album covers, are kept by collectors and worth thousands of dollars, the digital version of the audiobooks, comics, etc., are also collected by collectors. Not just books, but recently even the original copy of the first tweet by the founder and CEO of Twitter was sold for 2.9 million dollars to the highest bidder by using NFTs.

Sports: Sports have also started venturing into the world of NFTs. NBA sold the first sport-based NFT. Using NBA top shots, collectors can purchase the original highlight of an image or a sequence of play by their favorite players during an NBA game. Formula 1 and boxing are both launching their own NFT collections. 

Domain Names: It is also possible to purchase and collect domain names. The ownership of the domain name is stored in the NFT. One of the common ways of buying and storing names is through the Ethereum names service. 

Finance: NFTs, though popularly used for their superficial purposes, can be helpful too. This provides the NFT holders with value for holding the NFT in itself. For example, if an NFT allows the holder to enter an exclusive pool of investors, the said NFT can be traded or sold for that right. Their holders can also stake NFTs to earn passive income. Some NFTs also double as discount coupons or combos, such as in the case of BAKE coupons. These financial NFTs can also have artwork attached to them, but their value comes from their use, not their art. 

Music and v\]Video: Just as it is possible to attach an NFT to an image file or a GIF, it is also possible to link NFTs to audio and video files. By connecting the NFT to an audio file or album, musicians and video makers can start selling the original versions of their products. Just because a person holds the NFT to a song or video does not mean they have the copyright to it. It continues to be with the artist. Currently, the recording studios and other intermediaries earn from songs and music videos; using NFTs would diminish the need for these third parties, thereby increasing the income for the musicians. Kings of Leon was the first central rock band to release a digital version of their album; each album copy was uniquely numbered with NFTs. 

Real-World Asset: Real-world assets, such as shares, deeds, contracts, etc., could also use NFTs. Forgery and fraud are commonplace in the physical form. However, blockchain technology could prove its authenticity if these assets were given proper NFTs. In 2021, an NFT was created, which included the ownership of a property in California. Whoever owns the NFTS also holds ownership of the said property. It could also help prove the ownership of valuable items such as jewelry.

High fashion is another probable use case for NFTs. Most luxury fashion has no purpose other than the identity and exclusivity its ownership offers. This makes it a very logical use for NFTs. People can buy digital fashion products, such as digital sneakers or sunglasses, which are authenticated using NFTs. Big fashion houses have already started producing NFT-based digital products. A luxury virtual hoodie by Overpriced was sold for 19,000 pounds. 

Licensing: NFTs could be of great use in the software licensing sector. Firstly they would bring down piracy in the industry as it is tough to forge your ownership details when stored on NFTs. Secondly, NFTs allow people to sell their licenses directly to others who need them. So people won’t be stuck with licenses or year-long memberships. They could go to an NFT marketplace and sell it to someone who needs it. 

# How To Safely Buy NFTs And Where?

Non-fungible tokens, like other crypto tokens, are unavailable for sale and purchase on regular stock exchanges. Some specific platforms and businesses deal with NFTs. Some of them have been created specifically for this purpose. The process of purchasing an NFT is straightforward and can be done by following a few steps.

Purchase Cryptocurrency: The first step in purchasing an NFT is cryptocurrency. NFTs are based on blockchains, each of which has its cryptocurrency. NFTs must be bought by paying for the value in the blockchain cryptocurrency. It is not possible to purchase NFTs directly using fiat currencies. They must be first converted to cryptocurrencies. If one wants to get an Ethereum blockchain-based NFT, one purchase ether, and if it is a Binance smart chain-based NFT, one must purchase Binance coins. Cryptocurrency can be bought on various crypto exchanges like Binance, Coinbase, etc.

Transfer Crypto to a Wallet: The next step is to transfer your ether or other cryptocurrencies to a wallet like MetaMask. These wallets store access to cryptocurrencies and provide security from hacking. It is also possible to directly use the crypto to pay for the NFT if it is bought on the identical blockchain, for example, if purchased on Binance.

Choose an NFT Marketplace: The next step is to choose the marketplace. There are many marketplaces that one can choose from. Each of these marketplaces has its features, transaction costs, and other characteristics. One must consider these all before selecting the marketplace they want to purchase their NFTs.

Attach Your Wallet to the Marketplace: The next step is for the buyer to attach the wallet in which they have their cryptocurrency to the market. This allows the cryptocurrency to be transferred from your wallet to the seller’s wallet via the market.

Choose the NFT: One of the most critical steps in this process is choosing the NFT that one wants to purchase on the platform. Thousands of different NFTs are available on these platforms, and the market is growing daily. Choosing an NFT is a crucial decision and must be made in an informed manner as it is a long-term investment decision.

Purchase for the NFT: The final step is to purchase the NFT you selected. Purchase on platforms can happen in two ways either they can be done in an auction format, and the highest bidder gets the NFT, or the seller can quote a price, and the buyer pays the amount to purchase the product. Once the cryptocurrency is transferred, the transaction is stored in the blockchain, and the ownership changes to the new person.

This is the usual manner in which NFT purchase takes place. However, we have also seen recently that some real-life auction houses have started selling digital art pieces through auctions.

# How to Choose an NFT?

Choosing an NFT should be an informed decision that depends on the following factors:

Price: The first and most important factor before choosing an NFT is the price at which it is sold. One must look at their budget and then decide which NFTs are in their budget that they can buy.

Blockchain: Another factor that must be considered is the blockchain on which the NFT is minted. Most NFTs are on Ethereum; however, other blockchains also offer the minting process. One should choose a trustworthy and well-established blockchain.

Rarity: The rarity of an NFT is the factor that impacts the future price of the NFT. For example, the alien punks in crypto punks are worth more now than the human punks because there are only nine strange punks.  

Creator: Like when purchasing an actual art piece, digital art NFTs should also be bought according to who the piece’s creator is. One could invest in an artist’s NFTs, who they believe will become popular and famous.

Type: The type and kind of NFT are also necessary. Some people use NFTs as digital assets, some as collectibles or game purchases, while others treat them as financial instruments.

# How to Choose an NFT Marketplace?

Choosing the correct NFT marketplace is also very important. When selecting the NFT marketplace, it is essential to consider the following:

Token standard: Multiple different token standards are used for making NFTs. Depending on the NFT you want to purchase, you should check whether the marketplace offers NFTs of that token standard.

Transaction cost: Every time one purchase or sells an NFT, a transaction takes place, and it is recorded on the blockchain. For every transaction, the marketplace charges a fee or a commission. This rate of the price differs from one marketplace to another and should be considered when deciding on a

Type: There are different types of marketplaces. Some are universal and allow all kinds of tokens to be traded, while others only offer a specific ticket.

Verification process: Marketplaces have verification processes for the creators who want to sell their NFT on the marketplace. This must be a stringent process to restrict the presence of fakes and fraudulent NFTs.

Pricing method: The Pierce discovery process also depends on one marketplace to another. In some, it is done through an auctioning process, while in others, it is quoted by the seller or creator.

# Chapter 7: The Blockchain Technology

# What is a Blockchain?

Blockchain is often confused with the first-ever cryptocurrency - Bitcoin. This is because the term blockchain became popular only after the inception of Bitcoin. In reality, blockchain is the primary technology that enables the working of cryptos. If not for blockchain, we won’t be seeing any of the cryptocurrencies that are in circulation today. It is essential to know that cryptocurrency is just one of the various potential applications of blockchain technology.

In simple terms, a blockchain is a distributed electronic ledger built around a Peer 2 Peer system. This ledger can be shared among infinite users for their reference. The transactions entered into the blockchain are tamper-proof and time-stamped individually. A set of valid transactions makes a block complete. Each verified block is linked to the previous block to form a chain of blocks. Hence the name - Blockchain!  

The working of the Blockchain is different from that of centralized systems. It is a distributed ledger and works as a consensus-based network, thus, making it decentralized. Following is the step-by-step working of a transaction on the Blockchain.

1.  A node, a computer in the blockchain network, receives a transaction from the sender. It initiates that transaction on the web by creating it. Later, the cryptography technique gets the transaction digitally signed with a private key.
    
2.  The transaction information is passed from that node to all the other nodes on the network to validate the transaction based on some criteria preset by the Blockchain. This process is called the Gossip protocol. In technical terms, it is called the Proof of Work, the standard consensus algorithm of Blockchain. According to the algorithm, the nodes must solve a complex mathematical puzzle to authenticate a transaction. Usually, two or more nodes are required to validate the transaction.
    
3.  Once the nodes validate the transaction entirely, it is included inside a block and is propagated toward the leading blockchain network. This is when the trade gets its first confirmation.
    
4.  Later, when a new block is created for the same transaction, it goes through the same process above and gets linked to the first block, referred to as the “genesis block.” The league gets its first confirmation, and the transaction reaches its second confirmation.
    
5.  The same process is repeatedly reconfirmed for every new block created. Typically, about five-six confirmations are required for the transaction to be officially complete. And finally, the ownership of the cryptocurrency/digital asset is moved to the receiver.
    

# Smart Contracts

One of the most fundamental purposes of using a blockchain is that no intermediary is involved. Because blockchain is decentralized and all the functions that the intermediaries do are replaced with complex computer algorithms. A revolutionary application of blockchain that can potentially eliminate many intermediaries is known as Smart Contracts.

Smart Contracts can be defined as computerized agreements stored in a blockchain network. In smart contracts, all contract terms can be pre-coded, and the execution of these contracts can automatically be done once the contract conditions are met. Since these Smart Contracts are stored in a blockchain network, the security, transparency, and accessibility of the contract are increased exponentially.

Also, these smart contracts are immutable. They are tamper-proof, and no one can change the terms once the contract is activated. If any parties attempt to alter the contract terms, the agreement will get rejected while intimating all the parties involved. More importantly, these contracts are cost-effective as we can eliminate fees to typical intermediaries such as authorizers, document creators, etc.

Blockchain technology has become very popular over the last decade, primarily thanks to significant cryptocurrency investments. Blockchain is the technology on the basis on which cryptocurrency is based. Many people hear about it and need clarification or think it is a complex technology well beyond their understanding; however, that is untrue.

In simple terms, a blockchain is a database or ledger used to store information. It is also called distributed ledger technology (DLT). A database is data stored on a computer or electronic device that is organized to allow it to be used and viewed easily. Data up until recently was held in the form of databases, but that has changed with the advent of blockchain technology.

In a blockchain, data or information is stored in groups known as blocks. Whenever an alliance with a finite storage capacity becomes full, it gets chained to the previous block. Then, a new partnership is created, creating a long chain of blocks of information stored in that chronological order, known as a blockchain. Each new block is added after its storage fills up, and the chain continues to grow longer and longer. 

Blockchains are different from the other traditional forms of databases in that, while other databases store their data as a whole, the data in a blockchain is kept together in smaller units. Another key difference is that blockchains store their information with timestamps, which means we always know when that data was entered into the system. It ensures that the time entry related to data is valid. In effect, a blockchain also acts as a timeline of data. 

Another critical component that makes blockchain different is that it is decentralized, which is apparent from its “decentralized ledger technology.” While other databases need servers stored in a single place owned and operated by a single party, In contrast, blockchain holds its data over multiple servers known as nodes. This way, any single party does not control the data, and every time a new data entry is made, it is duplicated over all nodes using predetermined algorithms. blockchain

Due to its features that make it an authentic, transparent, and non-editable database, blockchain is slowly being adopted by many companies and governmental organizations.

Blockchain is one of the biggest game-changers the technology sector has seen in the past few years. According to the World Economic Forum's Future of Jobs report, blockchain will be one of the largest employment generators over the next five years till 2025. Blockchain is predicted to bring a significant change in the way many industries work, such as the Finance Industry, Banking Industry, Retail industry, Supply Chain industry, and even Governmental Administration.

# History of Blockchain

The idea of having a decentralized ledger with data stored in blocks has existed for a few decades. However, a developer (or developers) created the first real blockchain with the pseudonym of Satoshi Nakamoto in 2008. The following year, in 2009, Satoshi, for the first time, used Blockchain practically to maintain a public ledger for keeping track of the transactions of Bitcoin. It was then that Bitcoin was invented.

The first use of Bitcoin for a purchase happened in 2010 when a Florida-based man paid 10,000 Bitcoins for two pizzas from Papa John’s.

The use of blockchain exclusively as a ledger for storing cryptocurrency transaction details continued until 2014. This is called the first stage of blockchain. After 2014, the second stage of blockchain technology started. During this stage, the uses of blockchain moved past being just for cryptocurrencies. In 2014, Ethereum started creating computer codes that acted as financial instruments called smart contracts. The same year, R3, a group of blockchain companies, was formed to explore the different areas where blockchain can be used.

In 2015, NASDAQ and Chain, a blockchain company, came together to test blockchain to trade shares of a private company. After 2016, companies like Facebook and IBM started working with blockchain technology.

# Types of Blockchains

Public Blockchains: These are the most common type of blockchains. As the name suggests, Public blockchains are open to everyone, and anyone can access the data on these kinds of blockchains. Public blockchains are the most transparent form of blockchains. Public blockchains are used to track the transactions of the cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin and Litecoin. These blockchains are slightly slower than private blockchains since there are more nodes in a public blockchain, and the transaction must be recorded in every node.

Private Blockchains: Private blockchains are the other primary type used blockchains. In a private blockchain, the number of users is restricted. Only the people authorized to have access to the blockchain can see the data on the blockchain and record transactions on the same. Organizations and firms can have private blockchains to store data, with only people from within the organization having access. Private blockchains are much faster than their public counterparts; however, since the entire point of blockchain technology is to have multiple nodes through which verification is confirmed, private blockchains are not considered as secure as public ones.

Hybrid Blockchains: Hybrid blockchains are a mix between a public blockchain and a private blockchain, so they try to have the advantages of both public and private blockchains. In a hybrid Blockchain, some of the data is open to the public, and the rest of the data that may be more delicate can be kept only to be accessed by a select few. This makes the blockchain faster and more secure at the same time. 

Federated Blockchains: Federated blockchains are also called Consortium blockchains. They are similar to private blockchains, but instead of one organization, in a federated blockchain, access is given to multiple organizations that are part of a larger body, such as the government. 

# Critical Components of a Blockchain

To understand how a blockchain works, one must first know what a blockchain is made of. The components of a blockchain are:

Blocks: The blocks are the most basic units of storing information on a blockchain. After each block is full of data, it is connected to the previous block, thereby creating a chain of data that we call a blockchain. Blocks themselves consist of multiple parts. These parts are:

Data: This data has been stored on the block for safekeeping.

Nonce: A 32-bit whole number is generated for every block when creating a new partnership. This nonce then creates the hash.

Hash: The hash is a 256-bit number unique to every block. This hash encrypts the data on a block and connects one block to another in a given blockchain.

Block header: The block header provides us with the metadata related to the block, such as the hash of the previous block, the time stamp, and the nonce. 

Transactions: The block contains all of the transactions that have been stored on the block.

Nodes: Nodes refer to the people or servers with whom the blockchain has been shared. A blockchain has no central authority, and data is stored on distributed ledgers. Each of these ledgers has its node. The more the number of nodes in a blockchain, the more secure it is. Since the same data is secured on every node, the hacker will have to access each node and then change their data to hack into it or make a change.

Miners: Miners are the people who are responsible for adding new blocks to a blockchain. To add a new block to a blockchain, one needs to solve a complex mathematical problem; this problem can only be solved by trial and error and hence requires significant computing power. A nonce is a 32-bit number, and the hash is a 256-bit number, so about 4 billion nonce-bit combinations are possible. The process of finding the correct combination is called mining. For undergoing this process, the miners are rewarded in cryptocurrencies.

# Principles of Blockchain

Blockchain technology works based on certain principles; these principles are:

Distributed Database: The most basic principle behind blockchains is that the database in a blockchain is not stored in a single location and is spread across various nodes and servers. It means that for a transaction to be entered into a blockchain, it must be entered in every node, making it a very secure form of data storage, albeit a bit slower.

Peer-to-peer Transactions: The transactions and communication in a blockchain happen directly between the peers and nodes and not via a moderator. This ensures a certain amount of trust and transparency between blockchain network users.

Transparency: The transactions stored in a public blockchain are transparent. Anyone can access these transactions, making it harder for people to cheat or defraud others by creating false entries.

Irreversibility: The transaction, once stored on the blockchain, is marked with a timestamp, and it is stored as such. The data, once entered, cannot be changed after that. The entire chain must be mined again to make even a small change on one of the blockchain blocks. This would require massive computing power and would be nearly impossible.

Computational Logic: The data is stored on the blocks in a logical format, so the users don’t have to add it manually. They can create algorithms to enter the transactions by themselves. 

# Cryptography in a Blockchain

A blockchain uses two basic kinds of cryptography: the asymmetric key and the hash function. These two are used at the same time and have different processes.

The asymmetric key means two types of keys are needed to access the blockchain. For every blockchain, there is a public key and a private key. The public key is available to everyone on the network, and it is the same for everyone. However, the private key is unique to every node and is only available to the node owner. In this kind of cryptography, the public encrypts the plain data into cipher text. The node owner then uses the private key to decipher the reader to plain text. Combining the private and public keys creates a signature unique to the node owner. 

The hash function is the cryptography used for chaining the blocks to each other. Every block has its hash; at the same time, it also carries the previous block's hash. E.g., block number 21 in a blockchain will have the hash of block number 20, which will chain it to the last block. Meanwhile, block number 22 will have the hash of block number 21; this is how they connect and create a chain. It ensures no new person can create and attach random data to a blockchain.  The block’s hash is made via the Merkel tree, where every transaction has its hash, which keeps combining until we get a hash for the whole block.

# How do the Transactions go Through?

\-          The process of transactions being recorded on the blockchain goes through the following procedure:

\-          The transaction is requested by the people who want to purchase a cryptocurrency.

\-          The transaction is broadcasted via the peer-to-peer network to all the nodes.

\-          The node owners then validate the transaction on their records

\-          The node owners are compensated in the form of cryptocurrency.

\-          The transaction is then recorded on the block that is open at the time being.

\-          Once they are filled with transactions, the blocks are attached to the blockchain.

\-          The transaction is completed and timestamped.

# Fundamental Properties of Blockchain

Some of the fundamental properties of a blockchain are:

Distributed: One of the most essential features of blockchains is that they have a distributed ledger. They are also called distributed ledger systems for the same reason. It means a blockchain has multiple nodal centers instead of having a single central authority or a central node from where the data is disseminated. The data is hence stored on all these servers at the same time. These various nodes ensure the security of the data since it is nearly impossible to mutate the data on all nodes simultaneously. This also reduces the cost of the database since there is no need to have large servers on which the data is stored, thereby saving the cost of purchasing these servers and the rent of physically holding all these servers in a place.

Immutable: The data in a blockchain is immutable, i.e., the data is permanent in nature, and it can’t be mutated. This is so because once the various nodes verify the transaction on a blockchain, it is stored on a block, which is then attached to the chain. To change the data on a block, one needs to have the correct nonce and hash combination, which is one out of four billion and can only be found through the hit and trial method. Further, each block has its hash in the next block, so to change one block, one needs to change the data in every one of the blocks in the chain. 

Timestamped: One of the defining features of blockchain technology is that all of the transactions stored on the platform are time-stamped. This means the time and date are also simultaneously attached as soon as the transaction is entered on the block. This ensures that no one can backdate and change the values. It provides us with a record in chronological order. This feature allows us to keep track of all cryptocurrency transactions and ensure that the cryptocurrency is not doubly sold or bought.

Unanimous: The data stored on the blockchain platform is unanimous because everyone on the forum is verified and accepted through all nodes. One of the nodes may carry a different value or transaction, and the other takes an additional weight or a trade. Due to this feature, everyone has access to the same data, reducing the risks of discrepancies and errors in communication.

Anonymous: The transactions recorded on the blockchains do not carry the name or identity of the people involved. Most of these transactions are either anonymous or pseudonymous. This allows people to carry on transactions without associating themselves with it and, to a certain extent, pass various laws and regulations. The transactions cannot be traced back to the people.

Secure: The data history on a blockchain is considered incredibly fast. Since the data is not stored on a single server and the same data is stored on multiple nodes across the network, it is tough for someone to hack into the blockchain network and change the data at every node. In a traditional database, central servers are easy to break into; however, this is not so in a blockchain. Hence, blockchains are much more secure than the usual servers.

Programmable: Blockchain is a programmable data-storing service. This means the data must be manually put into the servers only sometimes. One can set up mathematical and logical algorithms that verify the transactions themselves. It also allows for setting up intelligent contracts over blockchain technology, a feature now increasingly used by blockchain companies such as Ethereum.

# What Industries Can Blockchain Revolutionise?

Banking Industry

Banking is one of the biggest industries that blockchain technology will completely transform. Banks, as of right now, keep all of their data on central servers, which are susceptible to data hacks and largescale monetary hacks, which result in losses worth billions of dollars every year. Once the banks start storing their transaction histories on a blockchain, they will be provided additional security. Further, another added advantage for the users or customers of these banks would be that the data would be transparent and accessible to all.

When such data is available to the public, it reduces financial irregularities, and the kind of banking failures that have happened over the years, such as the Lloyd brothers, will not happen again. 

Some of the uses of blockchains in banking would be:

*   Payments across borders will be much faster.
    
*   Easier loans will be provided through peer-to-peer loans instead of using central banks.
    
*   The clearance systems being used by the banks would be more rapid as well.
    
*   The use of cryptocurrencies as a form of parallel payment system along with fiat currencies.
    

Government Record-Keeping Sector

The record-keeping industry, especially in the government sector, would benefit heavily from the advent of blockchain technology. Since the data recorded in blockchains are immutable and permanent, along with timestamps, it would ensure that false certificates and records regarding identification, ownership, death, birth, and property transfer are not furnished. This would also make it easier to access these certificates and information. 

The transparency of data afforded through blockchain technology would mean that everyone would have the option of looking at this data, and it would not be secretive, meaning that people can easily verify if the government records being provided to them are authentic. This would decrease benefit fraud, and government facilities could be better targeted toward those who need them.

Health Care Sector

Data storage is one of the most significant issues faced by the healthcare sector. There are multiple forms, documents, and records that patients must fill out repeatedly in different hospitals every single time. This issue can be quickly resolved with the use of blockchain technology. Further, this way, all the hospitals can access the older records of their patient’s illnesses and injuries from a transparent and authentic open source.

Worldwide, more and more doctors and medical professionals are advocating for universal health care that includes all kinds of information required by doctors from their patients in a digital form. This universal health care can be achieved via the use of blockchain.

Media and Entertainment Sector

The media industry has changed significantly over the past decade with the advent of online media platforms such as Netflix, Amazon Prime, and YouTube. Using blockchain technology in the field, we could better track the payments made to purchase these accounts and further prevent instances where multiple people use it through a single charge. 

It will also help the companies in the media field to keep track of their records as to how and when a product, such as a song, film, etc., was released and how the actual reception was, without the tampering that is done by the promoters. Unique views or impressions will be counted via blockchains, not duplicated ones.

Supply Chain Sector

The supply chain is a sector that will be different once blockchain is ultimately adopted and integrated into it. Concerns about the sourcing of products from across the globe have made it necessary for global companies to trace where each product comes from and who its producers are. Consumers have become increasingly conscious about not using products sourced from places employing child labor or violating labor laws. For this purpose, blockchain technology can keep track of such data. Through it, everyone will also have access to it to ensure that the corporation provides the data correctly or not. Blockchain will also enhance the streamlining of the supply chain. Since if a producer can trace the supply chain to the manufacturer of the products, then he may reduce wastage.

Transportation Sector

The transportation sector in itself will be a great benefactor of the adoption of blockchain technology. In the airline sector itself, we see that there are multiple checkpoints at which payments are made, such as the agents, the insurers, the passengers, and the airlines. With blockchain, the costs will be settled much faster, thereby increasing the ease of making bookings. Further, it could increase the efficacy affected by the overbooking process since everyone can see the number of bookings from a single central space. 

A blockchain system can be set up that provides a single factor of identification that goes through all the different steps at the time of boarding, reducing the time for the passenger and the travel company. Blockchain will also help lower the redundancies and discrepancies in data collection in the shipping and cargo sectors.

Agricultural Sector

With the addition of blockchain technology to the agriculture sector, we could see a significant change in how logistics is handled. Firstly it would help us ensure that only the best quality crops are being provided to the consumers and that they are healthy. It will also help keep track of where the produce came from in case their quality could be better. One of the most significant issues that farmers face is that their payments at times are delayed, and they need these payments to secure seeds to sow for the next season; without these payments, their season could be affected heavily, with blockchain the prices will become almost instantaneous which would immensely help the farmers.

Retail Sector

The retail sector could use blockchain technology in multiple ways, the major one being to keep track of the stock. This easily accessible data option would allow everyone to see how much inventory is being used and at what rate; this way, the restocking process can be scheduled appropriately, thereby saving the retail company costs of stockpiling. 

Further, in the retail sector, it would make it easier for the customers to verify the authenticity and safety of the products offered at the retail store and that they are ethically sourced. Having transparent supply chains visible to suppliers also helps resolve disputes between them and the retailers faster and saves legal costs.

Insurance Sector

In the insurance sector, blockchain applications are trying to increase operational efficiency. One of the significant differences with other sectors is that in this sector, the technology is not aimed at creating new products or services but rather at cutting down costs, increasing the speed of service, and providing better customer service. By having a transparent and permanent record, the industry will also reduce instances of insurance fraud or multiple insurances. At the same time, it is also helpful to the insured parties as it will allow the person to get quicker claims and payouts from the insurance companies, which, if delayed, can significantly impact these people’s lives. Smart contracts, an application of blockchain technology, would also help reduce the time and costs of the underwriting processes.

Real Estate Sector

The Real estate sector will get a boost after introducing blockchain technology in its record-keeping and payment processes. In real estate, the trust between the buyer and seller is imperative; the buyer must be assured that the seller is the actual owner of the property and does not have any incumbent interest in it, and the seller must be guaranteed that the buyer is a genuine buyer who will pay him wholly for the said property. This trust is enhanced with blockchain technology's decentralized, transparent data-keeping methods. Further, through safer and faster payment methods, the cost of transferring the property will be significantly reduced, benefiting both parties. Companies like Safewire use blockchain technology to minimize wire fraud prevalent in real estate transactions.

Pharmaceutical Sector

The pharmaceutical sector has fake medicines, expired patents, and long testing periods. Blockchain technology can help resolve most of these issues. Firstly, having an open and transparent ledger would make it easier for consumers to check whether the medicines sold are authentic and have not expired. Further, the high cost of most drugs is because of the patents that have been claimed on them by pharmaceutical companies through blockchains; one could easily keep track of which all patents are expiring soon enough and thus provide a generic version of the same medicine to the consumers at lower prices and consequently save many lives. The testing phase can also be bettered with blockchains as if and when there are errors, they can be caught quickly and then be traced back to the same batch of medicine.

Education Sector

In education, people often provide fake degrees, diplomas, and certificates. This can be checked by ensuring all certificates are on a public blockchain platform. The educational institutions can confirm and verify that the proper authority has issued the provided certificates and belong to the right person. Further, credentials can be lost or damaged, and even carrying them from one place to another is an issue; by having them on a secure and authenticated platform, the students and degree holders can only take them physically, sometimes for authentication. Blockchains would also help the working of libraries, as it helps keep better track of the books being lent and who has which book, allowing even the set up of peer-to-peer libraries.

Stock Trading Sector

Much like how transactions regarding cryptocurrencies are stored on blockchain technology, the whole asset trading sector can completely shift to blockchain technology. This sector is the closest to the crypto industry, for which blockchain technology was primarily created in the first place. Using the transactions that are time-stamped, it is straightforward to find who is the owner of a particular share and if he has already sold it to another person or not. Further, if trading starts through blockchains, the trades will be settled much faster, and the seller can be credited with his money instantly. Through the use of algorithms and smart contracts, it would also become more accessible for stock traders to execute trades by themselves. 

Energy Sector

The energy sector has in the past been one of the most centralized sectors, where energy collection and distribution is done through power corporations, private or public, depending on the countries and territories. With blockchain, this could completely change with people moving on from these centralized power producers to acquiring power from decentralized sources, that is, peer-to-peer purchase of power, which reduces the intermediaries and, thus, the cost of energy and production. Also, using this technology, one could find the source of the point they are using, i.e., whether the energy they consume is clean or made with non-renewable sources. Some blockchain companies use energy certificates, which may be sold from one person to another and then redeemed for power. Another way that blockchains can impact the energy sector is the introduction of cryptocurrencies to pay energy bills. 

Food And Beverage Sector

Food and beverages typically have a concise shelf life and, if spoilt, can cause many diseases, such as salmonella or diarrhea. Via blockchain, one can track how old a specific food product is, and the consumer can then make an informed decision. The food supply chain can easily be monitored via blockchains. By using smart contracts, companies can also automate the orders for more products, thereby increasing efficiency and decreasing the workload. Also, if an issue arises, it is easier to trace where the raw material came from and where the problem might have occurred. 

Election Sector

Elections all over the world, and especially in developing countries, are marred by allegations of voter fraud. Elections would become much more secure once blockchains store the votes. This would happen in various ways. Firstly, when the voters' data is stored on a blockchain, it is transparent so everyone can see who are the voters in a particular area, how many of them have cast their votes. Further, verifying and tracing the vote means one can prove that the voices are not being hacked and changed to help a particular party or person. Also, blockchain companies are working on ways to start casting votes via blockchains themselves. Do we need to remind anyone of the chaos of the 2020 US presidential election?

Auditing Sector

The auditing process will become a lot easier once the data and information of the companies and firms are uploaded on the blockchains. This would make it difficult for the companies to falsify their accounts, and it would show an accurate picture of the monetary state of the companies. Further, it makes it easier to keep track of the purchases and sales made over the year by a firm, and with the time-stamped transactions, it becomes even harder to hide such expenses.

Mining Sector

The mining industry has started adopting blockchains as well. In the mining sector, there are multiple intermediaries. All of them can be brought on the same page quite quickly using this technology, which makes getting the mined product from the mines to the end consumers much more accessible. Further, the consumers can trace their products back to the mines from where they are claimed to be. This ensures that a certain amount of value is attached to the origin of the stone or metal. De Beers, the diamond company, has developed a blockchain solution called Tracr, which traces the source of the diamonds from the mine to the buyer. 

Public Assistance

Public assistance, with the application of blockchain, could reduce wastage significantly. This way, the Non-Governmental Organisations and other inter-government bodies can keep track of their assistance funds and verify that the aid's end receiver is the person for whom the support was intended or not. The World Food Programme of the united nations has already adopted blockchain technology for directly sending assistance to needy people by using blockchain authentication. In some countries such as Jordan, World Food Programme has attached the people’s biometrics to their blockchain-based accounts; once the person’s identity is confirmed, the money received via aid in the said account is automatically detected, thus allowing instant settlement at the grocery stores. 

Gambling Industry

The online gambling sector has grown by leaps and bounds; however, the biggest issue is the lack of transparency. This is something that the introduction of blockchain platforms can easily handle, as this would ensure transparency and inculcate trust between the business and the consumers. It removes the adage of “the house always wins” as the ledger stores all the data and is open. Decentralizing betting through blockchain also allows for anonymity which is necessary for numerous gamblers who do not want to disclose their gambling habits.

Ride-Hailing

Ride-hailing companies like Uber and Lyft rule this sector now; however, these companies represent a central authority or a mediator through which the driver is connected to the passenger. With the introduction of companies that use blockchain technologies, companies will offer a direct peer-to-peer network for ride-hailing. This would mean any central authority is not controlling that driver and is free to create his own market space and provide services as he sees fit at their rates. It is also beneficial to the passenger as the cost of the rides will be lower since the intermediaries’ commission is removed from the equation. Some blockchain ride-sharing apps, such as arcade city and Drive, are already available. The users can also make payments through cryptocurrencies on these apps. 

Charity

How charitable institutions handle donations will also change if they adopt blockchain to track the money in the institution. This way, the donor can follow exactly where the donated money has been utilized and that the institution has not used it for other purposes. This provides transparency in charity which could encourage more people to donate since many do not do it because they think that charitable institutions are corrupt. Various companies are working in this sector, such as Bitgive Foundation, a Bitcoin-based charity, and Givetrack, a blockchain-based donation platform with a permanent record of global charitable transactions. ‘

# Popular Blockchain Companies That Are Already Making An Impact

Since we know that cryptocurrencies are the most crucial blockchain applications, let’s discuss the top two cryptos in the world.

Bitcoin - Bitcoin is the original cryptocurrency and the first instrument to use blockchain technology commercially. It was started in 2008. Bitcoin is a peer-to-peer payment network through which you can pay another person without any central bank or authority. Bitcoin uses the blockchain technology underneath it to manage and keep track of the transactions. Bitcoin has popularized the concept of cryptocurrencies among the masses and currently has a market capitalization of around 950 billion dollars.

Ethereum \- Ethereum is one of the most famous blockchain companies and is credited with introducing the concept of smart contracts. Ethereum works in the financial services sector, allowing individuals to lend, borrow and save products via the Internet. It also runs ether, the second-largest cryptocurrency by market capitalization. Ethereum lets people conduct operations through its smart contracts.

Winding Tree: Winding Tree helps hotels and airlines by providing a decentralized platform for making travel bookings. Propy: Propy is a blockchain-based company that works in the real estate sector. It uses intelligent contracts created under blockchains to process purchases, sales, and real estate leases. Follow My Vote: Follow My Vote is a decentralized application platform that, as the name suggests, has been set up to follow the voice of every voter in a constituency; this provides transparency in the voting process and, at the same time, avoids voter fraud. It can offer services in all kinds of votes, within an organization, in the collegiate system, or even on a larger scale. 

Open Bazaar: Open Bazaar is an e-commerce platform based on blockchain technology. Unlike any other e-commerce platform, it acts as a peer-to-peer platform where one can sell anything they wish to sell and pay no fees as there is no central authority. The payments are made through cryptocurrencies, and transactions are recorded on blockchains. These purchases could be for anything ranging from art to everyday products. 

Consensys: Consensys is a blockchain software engineering company that provides end-to-end solutions to companies regarding blockchain technology. It has recently teamed up with Microsoft and Moët Hennesy Louis Vuitton to create blockchain software that will help them trace the luxury products produced by the company and its subsidiaries and provide for their authenticity. It also allows the customers to trace their products from design to purchase.

# How to choose a Blockchain? (For business owners)

Depending on the needs of a business or an organization, they may choose the blockchain. The decision to select the correct blockchain is dependent on the following factors:

Adaptability: The first factor when adopting blockchain technology to your business is checking whether the blockchain is applicable in your field. Blockchain requires information on servers that are open to everyone, so it might not be suitable for dealing with sensitive data. Further, blockchain is not a cheap technology, at least not yet. Hiring blockchain specialists is expensive and might not be possible for smaller-scale firms.

Scalability refers to the number of transactions handled on a single blockchain platform. If the business requires a large number of transactions, then it may choose a platform accordingly. While some platforms, such as Bitcoin, offer TPS (Transactions per Seconds) of only about 7 TPS, others, like Stellar, can go up to almost 1000 TPS. The scalability of a blockchain is measured on three factors: speed, security, and decentralization.

New vs. Existing: Organisations can decide that their needs require the use of blockchain technologies; they then have to choose whether they want to use an already existing blockchain platform or if they want to create their platform. Developing a new platform can be slightly more costly and time-consuming; however, it would be more personalized to the requirements.

Public vs. Private: There are two significant kinds of blockchain platforms, either private or public. Both public and private networks have advantages and disadvantages; while public networks are more secure, they are slightly slower. Private networks have fewer nodes, so they process transactions much faster, but they need to be more secure since it is easier for hackers to access fewer nodes.

Security: Security is of great concern when considering the blockchain platform, especially for firms dealing with sensitive information. One should look at the peer reviews of the forum they are choosing.

# Chapter 8: Investing in Cryptocurrencies

The cryptocurrency market has been relatively shorter than other traditional markets. However, it has expanded widely. This is solely because of the features cryptocurrencies have to offer. Bitcoin and every other cryptocurrency are known for their high volatility, giving massive gain opportunities for investors if they can digest the risk.

Cryptocurrency is an online market, and several digital platforms have simplified people to invest in this market. However, comparatively, it is much more complicated than acquiring traditional currencies. If you’re new to this domain and willing to invest in cryptos, here’s everything you need to know.

# Cryptocurrency and Investing - The Basics

There are thousands of cryptocurrencies present market, and figuring out where to put your money becomes intimidating. From a broader perspective, every cryptocurrency is highly encrypted, and most are decentralized. But Bitcoin is the first cryptocurrency most recognized in the market on blockchain technology. Hence, this makes Bitcoin one of the most reliable coins out there.

Many investors are inclined towards investing in only Bitcoin and a few other popular currencies. For instance, people are keen on investing in Ethereum, where they attempt to buy low and sell high, believing it can be the next Bitcoin.

In any cryptocurrency you invest in, there is a common denominator – volatility. A fact with cryptocurrencies is that they have value as long as people comprehend it to have value. Though technically accurate with any currency, cryptos are more significant as they are not backed by the government or precious metals, like other traditional currencies. Therefore, investing in cryptos comes with added risks.

# Getting Started

Investing in cryptocurrency means buying them and keeping them stored. So, to invest in cryptocurrency, we need to research where to buy and store the coins. Cryptocurrency exchanges are the places cryptocurrency can be purchased. They are online platforms that allow exchanging coins with fiat currencies or other digital assets.

There are several cryptocurrency exchanges out there on the internet. It is necessary to choose the right one as it is where you will buy, sell, trade, and store digital assets. Some popular crypto exchanges include Coinbase, Gemini, Binance, Kucoin, Kraken, Bybit, and Bitfinex. Apart from being popular, they are the most reliable exchanges available. There are exchanges where you can buy cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin and Ethereum using your bank account or a debit card. These are fiat-to-crypto exchanges, which allow the buying and selling of cryptos using only fiat currencies. In other words, you cannot buy a cryptocurrency with the help of another cryptocurrency.

The best site to research cryptocurrencies and exchanges is [https://coinmarketcap.com](https://coinmarketcap.com); if you have yet to go to this site, go there now and set up an account to do some detailed research.

If you live in the US, you must approach one of the exchanges that can convert your USD to Crypto, like Gemini or Coinbase. But they may not support and hold all the Crypto you want to buy and save. Then you might have to use another cryptocurrency exchange that allows cryptocurrency trading with other cryptocurrencies; you have to use an exchange like Kucoin, Kraken, or Binance.

Some exchanges facilitate traders to buy and sell cryptocurrencies for other cryptocurrencies. Technically, you can trade pairs like ETH/BTC and XRP/BTC rather than just BTC/USD or ETH/USD. Thus, you need not involve traditional currencies in buying and selling cryptocurrency.

Besides these two types of exchanges, another type allows participants to buy and sell their cryptocurrencies directly. LocalBitcoins was one of the oldest and most popular portals for direct one-to-one transactions of cryptocurrencies. In early 2023 they stopped providing Bitcoin trading services. The three largest peer-to-peer exchanges have closed, so be careful and never leave your funds on these or any sales. There are other sites, like Paxful, where the sellers set prices, not the exchange. However, this is the least preferred route to buy and invest in cryptocurrencies as it involves direct transactions with a stranger whose currency cannot be verified. So, if you’re new to cryptocurrency investing, starting with an exchange like Coinbase is recommended to exchange your USD to ETH or BTC to get started in Crypto.

Another option that’s recently getting more prevalent is Bitcoin ATMs. As per sources, there are over 4,000 cryptocurrencies ATM locations in seventy-six countries presently. These can be used to purchase Bitcoin and send it to your cryptocurrency wallet.

The term ‘cryptocurrency wallet’ is often used when storing coins. And that is because a cryptocurrency wallet is a device or software that allows users to store and retrieve their digital assets. And this wallet represents your portfolio. For instance, when you create an account with Coinbase, you automatically get a software wallet for yourself. If you purchase any cryptocurrency, it will be reflected in your Coinbase wallet. This amount can be used to send to other exchanges for crypto-to-crypto trading.

From the launch of Bitcoin, we have observed a bell curve movement in prices. Initially, it started slow, skyrocketed to $20,000 in 2017, couldn’t sustain, and dropped to around $3500. And this represents the volatility of Bitcoin.

So, investing in cryptocurrency is quite different from that of traditional investments. However, the risk analysis can be performed the same way. Like any other investment, you must weigh your potential profits against the risk you are willing to take. Investing in cryptocurrency comes from the type of trader you are. If you are a conservative investor looking to minimize risk and grow profits over time, cryptocurrency might not be the right choice for investment. Conversely, if you can bear the high risk to considerably maximize your earnings, you could try your hand in this market.

Bitcoin and altcoins are markets with inconsistent growth and significant volatility. Thus, ‘speculation’ goes more accurately with the above description than ‘investing.’

# How To Invest?

For a beginner just turning to the world of investing, the task behind actually starting to invest might seem daunting, even though it is pretty simple and easy to learn. Since purchasing cryptocurrencies is unlike investing in other assets available on stock exchanges or through your local banks, it takes a different approach. However, doing so only requires a minor procedure the investor can complete in a few steps. By just following these basic steps and complementing them with the necessary research, a person can start his journey into investing in Crypto Currencies. The steps are:

*   Preliminary research
    
*   Choosing the crypto-exchange
    
*   Creating an account on the crypto exchange
    
*   Choosing the cryptocurrency
    
*   Placing the order
    
*   Storing your Crypto investment
    

Preliminary Research

The first step, and one of the most important before making any investment decision, is preliminary research on the prospective assets or investments one will be looking into. This rule holds value in terms of crypto investments as well.

Regarding cryptocurrencies, the prospective investor should look forward to getting information on various topics such as the kind of market that is prevalent right now, the different types of cryptocurrencies that are being offered on the market, the rules and regulations regarding investments in cryptocurrencies, and crypto assets in the countries and territories they are staying in, how much they are willing to invest and finally but most importantly the various kind of risks that are evolved around supporting in this still a burgeoning sector of cryptocurrencies. 

It is unnecessary, but a person investing in cryptocurrency should also have a basic knowledge of the technology behind it - Blockchain. Investors should know how Blockchain works and how it secures their crypto transactions. The investor also should be aware of how cryptocurrency mining occurs. 

The person should also know what kind of patterns the particular cryptocurrency they plan to buy and the news and information regarding the company that has issued them. Also, knowing what kind of portfolio one wants to create is essential. It is usually intelligent to purchase several different cryptocurrencies to reduce the risk involved in this increasingly volatile market. 

Choosing the Crypto-Exchange

The next step toward owning cryptocurrencies is choosing the correct crypto exchange. We know cryptocurrencies are unavailable on regular stock exchanges or through your banks, although some have started offering cryptocurrencies. So, to trade or purchase crypto-currencies, the investor must approach a crypto exchange instead. 

Apart from crypto exchanges, it is possible to purchase cryptocurrency through crypto brokers such as Robinhood and SoFi. The decision to choose a crypto broker depends on various factors, such as the authenticity and security of the crypto exchange. These coins are being offered on that particular platform, the fee that the business will be charging, the method of payment that is available on the exchange such as - PayPal, bank transfer, credit card, debit card, and other crypto-currencies, and the user interface (UI) and user experience (UX) of the particular crypto exchange. 

Some of the major crypto exchanges in the world are Coinbase And Coinbase Pro, Cash App, Binance, Bisq, eToro, Gemini, and Kraken. One doesn’t have to stick to a particular crypto exchange since all the cryptocurrencies one wants to invest in might not be available at a single sale. One can have accounts on various crypto exchanges. 

Creating an Account on the Crypto Exchange

After deciding on a crypto exchange, the next logical step is to create your account on the said crypto exchange. A person needs to have a budget to make purchases of various crypto assets and keep track of their previous purchases. Although it depends from platform to platform, one usually must provide proof of their identification to fully set up their account before making any purchases per the various anti-money laundering (AML) and Know Your Customer (KYC) norms. This process is done to reduce the risk of fraud and fulfill the regulatory requirements of the country the person is residing.

Once such formalities are completed, one may be able to sell or buy cryptocurrencies on the crypto exchange. The person might be asked to provide a government-issued ID proof to the business, and since all of this process takes place online, they might even ask for a current photograph to match the details with the documents provided.

Choosing the Cryptocurrency

Choosing cryptocurrency is the most crucial step in purchasing cryptocurrencies and the investment decision. There are hundreds and hundreds of different cryptocurrencies that are available for purchase.

Some of the major cryptocurrencies that are currently being traded around the world are:

*   Bitcoin (BTC)  
    
*   Ethereum (ETH)  
    
*   Polkadot (DOT)  
    
*   Cardano (ADA)  
    
*   Ripple (XRP)
    
*   Binance Coin (BNB)
    
*   Litecoin (LTC)
    
*   Chainlink (LINK)
    
*   Stellar (XLM)
    
*   Internet Computer (ISP)
    
*   Filecoin (FIL)
    
*   Aave (AAVE)
    
*   Hedera (HBAR)
    
*   Algorand (ALGO)
    
*   Uniswap (UNI)
    
*   Near Protocol (NEAR)
    
*   Crypto Adoption Token (CAT)
    

One should decide on the cryptocurrency based on various parameters such as:

*   One should look at the coin's total market capitalization and cannot accessits supply and circulation. It helps you understand the cryptocurrency's size and a hint towards the stability it might offer. 
    
*   High fluctuations in the price of a cryptocurrency are not usually seen as a good sign. One should avoid coins that only climb in value because of one person or company endorsing them.
    
*   It is crucial that before buying a cryptocurrency, you study the persons and companies that have issued the said cryptocurrency. The kind of business the company is in, and the person's reputation can impact the coin's future value.
    
*   The white paper is the plan forward. It is created for every coin, and it contains the purpose of the particular cryptocurrency and the vision for the same. The investor should look at the white paper and compare what has been accomplished to what was promised. 
    
*   Technology: The underlying technology on which the cryptocurrency is based is also essential; disruptive technologies with a brighter future provide better value in coins, such as the innovative contract technology that Ethereum initiated.
    

Further, it must be understood that investing in just a single cryptocurrency is not the wisest decision. Hence it is necessary to build a portfolio of various cryptocurrencies based on your needs and what they offer to the investors. Ideally, an investor should have a healthy balance between the riskier but high-yielding coins and the stable but low-yielding coins. Initially, having your portfolio tilted more toward safer options is considered a better option. When one becomes more experienced, one can start investing in riskier coins. 

A good portfolio would have the following segments:

*   Store of Value: Bitcoin, Ethereum
    
*   Digital currency: Litecoin, XRP, XLM, dash, etc
    
*   Distributed applications: Polkadot, Cardano, Tron, Waves, etc
    
*   New tech: ADA, Tezos, Nano
    
*   Private coins: Monero, Verge, etc
    
*   Exchange tokens: Binance coin, Uniswap, etc
    
*   Stablecoins: Tether, DAI, USD coin
    

A healthy mix of the above shall give the investor an excellent risk-to-yield ratio, which shall be suitable for a long-term investment.
